 GOWDA: Goal-oriented Web Documents Querying tool Bahareh Zarei Technische Universität Chemnitz Chemnitz, Saxony bahareh.zarei@informatik.tu-chemnitz.de Martin Gaedke Technische Universität Chemnitz Chemnitz, Saxony martin.gaedke@informatik.tu-chemnitz.de ABSTRACT Each day, vast amount data published web. In addi- tion, rate content published growing, potential overwhelm users, particularly technically unskilled. Furthermore, users various domains expertise face challenges trying retrieve data require. They may rely IT experts, experts limited knowledge individual domains, making data extraction time- consuming error-prone task. It would beneficial domain experts able retrieve needed data create relatively com- plex queries top web documents. The existing query solutions either limited specific domain require beginning predefined knowledge base sample ontologies. To address limitations, propose goal-oriented platform enables users easily extract data web documents. This platform enables users express goals natural language, platform elicits corresponding result type using algorithm proposed. The platform also applies concept ontology semantically improve search results. To retrieve relevant results web documents, segments user’s query mapped entities ontology. Two types ontologies used: goal ontologies domain-specific ones, comprise domain concepts relationships among them. In addition, platform helps domain experts generate domain ontolo- gies used extract data web documents. Placing ontologies center approach integrates level seman- tics platform, resulting more-precise output. The main contributions research provides goal-oriented platform extracting data web documents integrates ontology-based development web-document searches. ACM, New York, NY, USA, 4 pages. https://doi.org/10.1145/3209280.3229099 1 INTRODUCTION The World Wide Web provides access vast amount unstruc- tured semi-structured information various forms, including web pages, research papers, scholarly documents, e-books. The current volume rate increase web documents causes major problems unskilled users attempting retrieve exact data need[12]. Conventional approaches searching web documents, including keyword-based form-based techniques, sufficiently expressive enable users formulate complex queries, also present limitations regarding identifying returning correct data. Although using web query languages, in- cluding XQuery WebSQL, increases expressiveness extent, languages require users certain amount technical knowledge[16]. Another limitation conventional search methods ignore domain semantics words. A word’s semantics may change depending domain used. For example, word “cell” means “basic unit life” biology domain “area radio coverage” cellular-network domain. To increase precision query results, essential consider user’s domain. To address above-mentioned problems, propose goal- oriented approach finding exact web data users need using ontology-based searches. Domain knowledge descri- bed form ontology consisting structured set domain entities relationships among them[11]. The advan- tages integrating ontologies web searches include increased precision, easier access data exacted, extraction concepts instead keywords[14]. Furthermore, proposed platform, GOWDA, enables users express intentions natural language form goal statements, helps naive users create reasonable search queries. A goal achieved set actions corresponding results. This fe- ature contributes solution’s simplicity expressiveness. In addition, overcome limitations imposed semantic ambiguity words, approach integrates domain ontologies middleware layer. After type results identified, goals’ entities, including subgoals tasks, mapped domain ontologies, GOWDA platform also enables domain experts generate. Because generating ontologies manually time consuming, propose semi-automatic technique generating domain-specific ontologies given domain. This method asks domain experts first prepare large dataset domain-related documents DocEng ’18, August 28–31, 2018, Halifax, NS, Canada B. Zarei M. Gaedke uses dataset identify main domain entities relationships among them. Then, Word2vec algorithm 1 used extract entities. After step, domain experts asked refine output. This remainder paper structured follows. Section 2 presents overview similar approaches. Section 3 describes proposed approach detail presents GOWDA plat- form’s overall architecture. Section 4 validates feasibility proposed approach. Section 5 presents conclusions discusses potential future improvements. 2 RELATED WORK In recent years, extracting data web documents received considerable research attention. A wide spectrum domains benefit improvements processes creating queries extracting knowledge web documents. One core domain enterprise one, requires business users ad- hoc access data. An example enterprise application focus business intelligence presented [17] used domain-specific, ontology-based architecture integrate data heterogeneous sources help ontology model, Business Ontology (BO), contained business-related concepts. The Semantic Query Designer top layer, application layer, architecture enabled users visually formulate query, mapping BO data-storage layer occurred. The proposed approach similar [17] ontology-based development central aspect it. Furthermore, proposed approach enables interaction based natural language. Early practices area focused domain-specific languages define rules data extraction, including solutions provided [9, 10, 13]. quality extraction rules defined operators determined quality data extracted. Other solutions used machine-learning techniques, inclu- ding supervised semi-supervised methods. In solutions, domain expert first creates sample dataset various web docu- ments trains tool it, training phase tool extract data [6]. Approaches [1, 3, 4] rely machine-learning techniques, meaning necessarily in- volve creation large training datasets, time-consuming task domain experts. GATE [2] well-known framework relies machine- learning techniques extract information provides rich toolkit natural language processing data mining. The proposed approach differs GATE takes account semantic meaning words. Other solution category gains popularity recent years, Natural-language interfaces Apple’s Siri 2, Cortana 3 Amazon’s Alexa 4. The proposed category. This approach classified ontology-based semantic search technique. In techniques, meaning query important, rather syntax keywords used [11]. The main aspect 1https://deeplearning4j.org/word2vec.html 2https://www.apple.com/ios/siri/ 3https://www.microsoft.com/en-gb/windows/cortana 4https://developer.amazon.com/alexa differentiates GOWDA approach existing ontology- based practices GOWDA approach integrates goal ontology layer better address user’s needs enable natural-language interface. 3 GOWDA FRAMEWORK AND IMPLEMENTATION We propose goal-oriented approach answer users’ queries effi- ciently. Goal-oriented designs aim create usable platform focusing users’ goals rather tasks [5]. As Fi- gure 1. shows, GOWDA architecture comprises three main components. The first Natural Language Processing (NLP) component, responsible translating user’s goal reasonable query performing Part speech (POS) tagging goal-ontology mapping. This component implemented using Python Natural Language Toolkit (NLTK) library. The second component goal ontology, responsible mapping output NLP component goal ontology (goal result). Then, type results user querying, person, location, domain-dependent concept, identified, mapping domain ontology performed. The goal ontology implemented Protege 5, open-source ontology editor, also used build knowledge base. The third architecture component mapping back- ground knowledge form ontology web documents. Each related web page semantically annotated according domain ontology. Semantic annotations formal way descri- bing knowledge contained web document form RDF files mapped domain ontology extract instances entity. For instance, ‘Jane Wilson’ ‘TU Chemnitz’ two instances extracted web page mapped student university entities, respectively, education domain ontology. As example, user first types text field “Who working Web engineering?” goal. Based output NLP component, “Who” tagged WP, refers WH pronoun corresponds person’s name. In example, desired result names people working “web engineering” field, so, results type DO:person (DO entities domain ontology). The SPARQL query language returns results entities labeled person value “web engineering” field. 4 ONTOLOGY GENERATION In order platform usable various domains, corresponding domain ontologies must integrated plat- form. In approach, generating ontologies includes three steps: preprocessing, concept extraction, relationship extraction. As primary task, domain experts upload sample domain-related documents serve text corpus later steps. Next, prepare corpus step 2, preprocessing performed, consists removing special characters stop words, latter defined frequently used words carry important meaning, including ‘an’, ‘and’, ‘for.’ Removing words provides accurate training set use step 2, concept extraction, 5https://protege.stanford.edu/ GOWDA: Goal-oriented Web Documents Querying tool DocEng ’18, August 28–31, 2018, Halifax, NS, Canada Figure 1: Platform overall architecture. extracts keywords according word’s semantic con- cept using Word2vec algorithm, two-layer neural-network algorithm. Word2vec selected use proposed approach unique performance providing syntactic semantic measurements words [19]. The word2vec algorithm trained large corpus text forms vector space repre- sentation corpus, onto input words mapped. To word corpus, word2vec assigns vector later used compute similarity words sentences. The proposed approach uses Python genism library 6for im- plementing Word2vec algorithm. Step 2, Concept Extraction, extracts main keywords corpus. Preparatory this, domain expert identifies small set seed concepts, then, algorithm extracts new concept candidates based con- cept set. After iteration, domain expert confirms relevant extracted terms key concepts deletes irrelevant ones. To increase quality model produced, training phase tunes two parameters, size min_count. The size para- meter dimensions feature vectors, min_count sets minimum frequency. For example, min count = 3 tells algorithm ignore words frequency lower 3. The values used parameters depend size training corpus. Finally, step 3, relationship extraction, consists identifying (entity, relationship, entity) tuples entities already extracted step 2. The overall approach relationship extraction first split text sentences tag sentence POS tags, including nouns, verbs, adjectives, etc. To explore semantic relationships among concepts, one possible approach self-supervised one[7], identifies textual patterns occur key concepts. However, approach considers verbs linking two concepts relationships. The detailed procedures shown Figure 2. 5 VALIDATION This section demonstrates feasibility approach. For demonstration, chose traveling domain presented specific scenario. First, constructed ontology based corpus provided domain expert, consisted 12,917 sentences included data regarding flight, hotel car rental histories. The corpus contains short sentences long textual descriptions. Using set 10 seed words provided algorithm 64% precision identifying key concepts. The output 6https://radimrehurek.com/gensim/ Figure 2: Ontology generation algorithms. domain ontology stored RDF format form triples <N1, P, N2>, example: <hotel, isLocatedIn, city>. Figure 3. illustrates detailed steps addressing query “What addresses hotels located Berlin 4-star ratings?”. According NLP component, combination “What” WH pronoun noun “addresses” location reference signified fulfill user’s goal, result consist instances domain entity. The entity type address defined location domain ontology. The next step identified key terms query term’s corresponding POS tags. To map terms corre- sponding entities domain ontology, query run ontology. The nouns mapped classes, verbs presented relationships among classes. For class, list interchangeable words user’s query terms could mapped. For instance, “accommodation” listed interchangeable word class hotel, so, “accommoda- tion” used query, platform mapped class hotel. Deﬁnition 1. The understandability set programming con- structs degree ease predicting results computa- tions deﬁned means constructs. JUSTIFICATION. The proposed deﬁnition appears similar deﬁnitions understandability given Grubb Takang (2003) : Program understandability ease program understood—that is, ability determine program works reading source code accompany- ing documentation. Patig (2008) : Understandability seen [...] aspect ease use, namely effort r equir ed r ead corr ectly interpr et model. If person predict results given computation (e.g. result function given values input parameters), person certainly understands piece code. The oppo- site implication also true, provided input re- sult over-complicated computations (for example, numbers many digits fraction part diﬃcult human cope with). It may claimed deﬁnition understandability given strongly depends input data: time complexity de- pends size input data. If time complexity high, easy predict results, even code fairly simple. This indeed true general, research reported form comparative study: always pair sets programming constructs (e.g. Visual Basic vs. new programming constructs), investigating whether one set easier understand other. In context, impact input data neglected, algorithm input data always experimental control groups. However, conducting type experiment impor- tant use input data require large number man- ual computations—otherwise, time spent participant calculations would considerably greater time necessary understand code. This would obscure difference experimental control version. Assumption 1. The understandability set programming constructs, evaluated group people, described following performance indicators (we call FACT indicators understandability): • F — ﬁrst attempt failure ratio: percentage people (partic- ipants) fail ﬁrst attempt predict results computation deﬁned means given set programming constructs; • A — attempt number: average number attempts needed predict correct results computations deﬁned code = containing analysed programming constructs (if A 1 F 0 —all participants group predict correct re- sults ﬁrst attempt); = • C — cancellation ratio: percentage participants unable complete given assignment (i.e. correctly predict result, regardless number attempts) given amount time; • T — prediction time: average time used participants predict results computation deﬁned code composed analysed programming constructs. 2. Understandability indicators In context programming constructs, understandability deﬁned follows. JUSTIFICATION. Prediction time, T , seems important natural indicator understandability. For computational tasks per- formed human, one assume mental process whole split two types activities: understanding given 208 M. Ma ´ckowiak et al. / The Journal Systems Software 142 (2018) 206–222 piece code (what I given step) perform- ing actual calculations. If two groups programmers given fact computational task (in sense arithmetical calculations, e.g. computing value similar polynomials), compu- tations described different programming constructs, time spent arithmetical calculations average similar groups, difference pre- diction time attributed activity understanding code. Thus, prediction time, T , seems good indicator under- standability. The second important understandability indicator cancel- lation ratio, C . It especially important context con- trolled experiments limited time. Some participants would need time allotted experiment. If one group cancellation ratio higher other, proba- bly means code diﬃcult understand. The two remaining indicators—ﬁrst attempt failure ratio ( F ) attempts number ( A )—are somewhat auxiliary, still pro- vide valuable insight. We may expect two groups par- ticipants given computation tasks expressed using different programming constructs, group F A lower fewer problems understanding program. 3. Experiment design The experiments involved following roles: lecturer (the per- son role provide introductory lecture—one authors this), observers (the purpose role make sure cheating experiments—two au- thors acted observers), participants split experimental control groups. Participants. The participants 114 ﬁrst-year students bachelor degree programme Management Engineering Poznan University Technology. A list courses may en- hanced mathematical computational skills presented Tab. 1 . The experiments conducted second half Information Technology Management course (the second semester curriculum) academic years 2014/2015 2015/2016. During ﬁrst experimental session 2015, conducted experiment presented Section 5 , three remaining ex- periments took place single session 2016. In lat- ter session, students solved tasks order presented following sections paper. The number participants three experiments decreased task task, able provide answers preceding tasks allotted time ran (the ﬁrst task done 44 participants, sceond—36, last—28). Before starting experiments, participants already undergone (during study programme) introduction pro- gramming Visual Basic: 10 hours lectures 15 hours lab- oratory classes. The course conducted regular tutors involved either experiments development constructs presented paper. The students attended experiments regular classes. Their results affect grades. Execution scenario. The procedure used experimen- tal sessions consisted following steps: 1. Lecture. This lasted 90 minutes consisted (1) intro- duction end-user programming, (2) review Visual Basic features, (3) explanation programming concepts end-users presented paper, (4) information experiment. The presentation included 103 slides: 23 end-user programming problem, 41 Visual Basic, 36 Board Programming, 3 experiment. The lecture attended majority prospective partici- pants. 2. Group selection. We used random stratiﬁed sampling form experimental control groups. We asked tutors course indicate participants good programming slow program- ming assignments. Half good programmers randomly assigned experimental group, half control group. The done slow programmers, identiﬁed either good slow. 3. Experiment. A week lecture main part exper- iment took place. It allotted 100 minutes, consisted following parts: (a) Review mini-lecture. The lecturer provided short review material presented lecture (ca. 30 minutes). It covered Visual Basic (8 slides) end-user pro- gramming concepts discussed (27 slides). Also, tech- nical aspects explained (how log system, ﬁnd slides used lecture, pro- vide feedback, etc.). All participants attended lec- ture. (b) Warm-up. Two exemplary assignments presented participant concerning Factorial function (in Visual Basic) Power function (in Board Programming). BACKGROUND Ontology tools applied stages ontol- ogy life cycle including creation, population, imple- mentation, maintenance ontologies (Polikoff, 2003). An ontology used support various types knowledge management including knowledge retrieval, storage, sharing (Pundit & Bishr, 1999). In one popular definitions, ontology “the specifica- tion shared knowledge” (Waterson & Preece, 1999). For knowledge-management system, ontology regarded classification knowledge. Ontologies different traditional keyword-based search en- gines metadata, able provide search engine functionality semantic matching. On- tologies able search efficiently traditional methods. Typically, ontology consists hierarchical descriptions important concepts domain descriptions properties concept. Traditionally, ontologies built highly trained knowledge engineers domain specialists may familiar computer software. Ontology construction time-consuming laborious task. Ontology tools also require users trained knowl- edge representation predicate logic. XML suited describe machine-understand- able documents interrelationships resources ontology (Gunther, 1998). Therefore, W3C recom- mended use RDF, RDFS, DAML+OIL, OWL. Since then, many tools developed imple- menting metadata ontologies using lan- guages. SPARK: Adapting Keyword Query Semantic Search Qi Zhou, Chong Wang, Miao Xiong, Haofen Wang Yong Yu Apex Data Knowledge Management Lab?? Department Computer Science Engineering, Shanghai JiaoTong University, 200240, Shanghai, P.R.China, {jackson, wangchong, xiongmiao, whfcarter, yyu}@apex.sjtu.edu.cn Abstract. Semantic search promises provide accurate result present-day keyword search. However, progress semantic search delayed due complexity query languages. In pa- per, explore novel approach adapting keywords querying semantic web: approach automatically translates keyword queries formal logic queries end users use familiar keywords perform semantic search. A prototype system named ‘SPARK’ implemented light approach. Given keyword query, SPARK outputs ranked list SPARQL queries translation result. The translation SPARK consists three major steps: term mapping, query graph construction query ranking. Speciﬁcally, probabilistic query ranking model proposed select likely SPARQL query. In experiment, SPARK achieved encouraging translation result. 1 Introduction In next stage web revolution, termed semantic web, web resources made available various kinds metadata described ontologies1. Cor- respondingly, many semantic query languages (e.g. RQL [1], RDQL 2, SquishQL [2] SPARQL 3) proposed querying ontologies. However, order use semantic query languages, end users master com- plex formal logic representations familiar underlying ontologies. This become critical gap semantic search end users [3][4]. Meanwhile, users accustomed traditional keyword search years. Therefore, valuable enable users carry semantic search inputting keyword query. However, keyword query diﬀerent semantic search. To adapt keyword query semantic search, overcome following obstacles: 1) Vocabulary Gap: Casual web users usually ?? This work funded IBM China Research Lab. 1 In paper, ontology refers knowledge base (KB) includes concepts, relations, instances instance relations together model domain. 2 http://www.w3.org/Submission/RDQL/ 3 http://www.w3.org/TR/rdf-sparql-query/ knowledge underlying ontology, words queries may quite diﬀerent ontology. 2) Lack Relation: Relations concepts/instances required explicitly stated formal logic queries, often missing keyword queries [5]. How automatically discover missing relations becomes big problem. 3) Query Ranking: Due ambiguity keyword query, may multiple formal queries pro- duced one keyword query. How rank queries big challenge [6]. Faced diﬃculties, present novel approach SPARK sys- tem. SPARK automatically translate keyword queries corresponding SPARQL queries domain ontology, aim adapting keyword query semantic search. The main translation steps are: term mapping, query graph construction query ranking. Term mapping maps terms key- word query resources knowledge base narrow vocabulary gap. After that, query graph construction links mapped resources missing relations concepts obtained complete query graph constructed. Finally, probabilistic query ranking model estimates likely SPARQL query among candidate queries. In way, end users keep habit typing keywords querying semantic web data transparently, increases social utilization semantic search. From evaluation 750 various keyword queries three ontologies, SPARK achieved encouraging MRR 4 score 0.677. The rest paper organized follows: Section 2 deﬁnes problem formal query construction. Section 3 details main steps formal query construction SPARK. The implementation experimental result pre- sented Section 4. Section 5 outlines related work. We give conclusion future work last section. 2 From Keywords Formal Query Answering many kinds semantic query formulated problem ﬁnding group objects connected certain relationships restrictions. In ontology, semantic query equal query graph con- strained object nodes property arcs. An example query “Find states mississippi river runs border texas” querying geography ontology (Fig. 1). It rewritten equivalent conjunctive formal logic expression: ?x ← (?x, is, State)∩(Mississippi River, runThrough, ?x)∩(?x, border, Texas) class ‘State’, instance ‘Mississippi River’ ‘Texas’ ontological re- strictions nodes ‘border’ ‘runThrough’ required connecting arcs pattern. ‘?x’ variable passing restrictions query. So reduce problem translating keyword queries formal queries 4 MRR: Mean Reciprocal Rank [16]. Fig. 1. Geography Ontology (Schema) problem constructing equivalent query graphs keywords. To clarify problem, give formal deﬁnitions follows: Knowledge base D : hC, I, L, R, τi directed graph GD where: C R deﬁne sets class relation. I L sets instance literal 5. Function τ : (C∪I)×(C∪I∪L) → R deﬁnes triples D. Additionally, use symbol resource {e} : {C ∪ R ∪ I ∪ L} represent classes, relations, instances literals. Keyword query K bag terms {t}. In assumption, end users knowledge underlying ontology arbitrary keyword queries issued users. 0i D graph GF subsumed 0 , R , L GD. V set variable nodes conjunct relations nodes. 0 ∪ C 0 ∪ V ∪ L 0 : (I τ , V, τ 0) → R deﬁnes triples F . Formal query F : hC 0 ∪ C 0 ∪ V ) × (I 0 0 0 , I From deﬁnitions above, formal query construction problem modeled as: Under knowledge base D, given keyword query K, constructing ranking candidate formal queries {F}(cid:22) likely query among highest ranked ones. For example, assume web user intends express information need “Show states mississippi river runs through? ”. He may type “mississippi river state” keyword query. In SPARK, formal query con- struction process map keywords knowledge base complete candidate formal query graphs D. In resolving ambiguity (term ‘mis- sissippi’ may refer ‘mississippi state’ ‘mississippi river’) information loss (lack relations among terms), many candidate formal queries diﬀerent senses construction results. The query ranking process estimates conﬁdence score candidate formal query likely formal queries ranked higher. Take formal queries Table 1 example, one assigned ranking score (Column ‘Total’). When ontology selected underlying knowledge base, ontology processing module automatically indexes resources. The formal query construction module takes keywords input, returns ranked list SPARQL queries output. Once user inputs keyword query, term mapping step uses group mapping methods ﬁnd corresponding resources knowledge base ac- cording user’s keywords. Then, query graph construction step enumerates possible query combinations applies Minimum Spanning Tree algorithm [7] construct complete query graphs diﬀerent senses mapped re- sources, missing relations concepts made query. Finally, query ranking step evaluates constructed formal queries two perspectives: keyword query model (KQM) knowledge base model (KBM). A ranked list SPARQL queries given back end user. In next three sections, detail term mapping query graph construction steps, model query ranking problem. Fig. 2. SPARK Framework Term Mapping Query RankingIndexingOntologyK.B.Query Graph ConstructionMappedResourcesQueryGraphsFormal Query ConstructionOntology ProcessingKeywordsSPARQL 3.1 Term Mapping The purpose term mapping ﬁnd corresponding ontology resources (i.e. classes, instances, properties literals) term keyword query. The names labels ontology resources used mapping. In im- plementation, two types mapping methods employed: 1) morphological mapping employs string comparison techniques stemming, Sub-String, Edit-Distance, I-Sub [8] ﬁnd morphologically similar words; 2) semantic mapping mainly utilizes general dictionaries like WordNet [9] ﬁnd seman- tically relevant words (e.g. synonyms). During term mapping process, term K matched knowledge base diﬀerent mapping meth- ods, e.g. term ‘river’ mapped two ontology resources: class ‘River’ direct mapping instance ‘Fall-River City’ Sub-String mapping. We assign pre-deﬁned conﬁdence value P (e|t)(P (e|t) ∈ (0, 1]) mapping method determine mapping quality. Generally, conﬁdence value direct matching higher synonym-based matching. Term map- ping associates term keyword query senses knowledge base. Therefore, term mapping process, term longer lexical string represents list resources indicating kinds elements user wants. In next section, query graph construction process construct candidate formal query graphs diﬀerent query senses mapped ontology resources. 3.2 Query Graph Construction The query graph construction process builds candidate query graphs ontology resources mapped above. Firstly, mapped resources split diﬀerent query sets. Then, Minimum Spanning Tree algorithm applied construct possible query graphs query set. Finally, query graph interpreted SPARQL query conversion rules. Fig. 3. There comes problem: pick likely formal query end users? In section, query ranking used solve problem. We model query ranking as: “In knowledge base D, probability constructed formal query F user issued query given keyword query K?”. That is, determine P (F|D, K): probability generating event F event D K, core idea treating query ranking problem conditional probability event. The challenge turns estimate prob- ability. Instead estimating probability directly, apply Bayes’ Theorem obtain P (F|D, K) = P (D, K|F )P (F ) (1) P (F ) priori probability formal query F . P (D, K|F ) prob- ability generating knowledge base keyword constructed formal P (D, K) query. We assume keyword query K knowledge base D in- dependent events. Accordingly, P (D, K|F ) divided two parts, P (F|D, K) written as: P (F|D, K) = P (D|F )P (K|F )P (F ) P (D)P (K) (2) Formula 2 intuitive, apply Bayes’ Theorem P (D|F ) P (K|F ) Formula 2)and obtain intuitive formula: P (F|D, K) = P (F|D)P (F|K) P (F ) ∝ P (F|D)P (F|K) (3) P (F|D) probability generating F D. P (F|K) probability generating F K. P (F ) priori probability F . In paper, assume formal queries uniform distribution P (F ) equal among candidate formal queries. Thus, query ranking model propor- tional two sub probabilistic models: keyword query model P (F|K) knowledge base model P (F|D). The next two sub-sections illustrate estimation two models SPARK. Keyword Query Model (KQM) The keyword query model reﬂects prob- ability generating formal query keyword query. Generally, two intuitive properties. – Mapping Proximity: The ranking function take keyword mapping proximity account. In keyword mapping, every term ti K, mapping probability introduced indicate similarity terms K resources D. A formal query resources higher mapping scores given higher priority lower mapping score. – Relevance Keyword Query: The ranking function rank for- mal queries higher relevant user’s keyword. The concepts mentioned formal query keywords user given higher score. Take constructed queries Table 1 example, third fourth queries reasonable perspective keyword query rest queries either user’s expressed resources add many resources knowledge base. We deﬁne captured information need K distance measurement K F . To calculate distance, resolve distance two features: keyword mapping proximity proximity(F, K) query relevance relevance(F, K): Keyword mapping proximity score proximity(F, K) bases average term mapping proximity P (e|t) score. A higher proximity(F, K) indicates F relevant user’s terms. Query relevance score relevance(K, F ) determined proportion common resources F K. The proportion common resources K reﬂects many resources F directly mapped K. And propor- tion common resources F reﬂects many resources mapped K contained F . In translating process, natural language query re-written mul- tiple short keywords query according understanding it. For example: keyword query ‘state ohio river ﬂow‘ translated natural lan- guage query ‘show states river ohio runs through?’. Then, translated keyword queries sent SPARK constructed SPARQL queries evaluated gold-standard SPARQL query created original natural language query. If constructed SPARQL semantically equivalent gold-standard one, considered wrong. We took two metrics evaluation: Recall MRR. Recall indicates whether SPARK construct least one proper SPARQL query can- didate queries MRR focuses overall performance SPARK. For set constructed SPARQL queries SPARK, computed per- formance Reciprocal Rank (RR) ﬁrst correct answer. If none SPARQL queries correct, score 0 given. Otherwise, score equal reciprocal rank ﬁrst correct one. We calculated mean recip- rocal rank test keyword queries MRR widely used evaluating question-answering tasks [16]. 4.2 Experiment Result We used 250 keyword queries ontology. In future work, consider enhance SPARK two ways: 1) enlarge query scope introducing structured operators (e.g. NOT, OR, etc) improving human interaction support translate complicated information needs. 2) extend SPARK’s approach multiple ontologies web-scaled usage. Introduction A recent literature survey research methods purposes characterizing re- search studies field end user development [1] shown field evalua- tions EUD systems relatively uncommon. Mostly, systems evalu- ated lab setting, approach useful sometimes necessary prerequisite field testing, disregards impact context actual use systems would deployed practice. Which measures taken outcomes expected? 2 This chapter discusses methodological questions, starting discus- sion actually constitutes ‘success’ case EUD deployments. Related work A recent literature survey [1] classified research methods used field End User Development (EUD), pointed significant part work performed field EUD (42%, works covered survey) includes evaluation EUD systems parts thereof. We start introducing TagTiles, host technology enabling tangible interaction, TagTrainer, EUD environment constructing interactive exercises. TagTiles TagTrainer TagTiles interactive board supports tangible interaction objects adorned RFID tags; see Figure 1. It encases grid RFID tag readers grid RGB LED lights provide visual stimuli feedback tangible in- teraction. The board detect placement, lifting movement objects surface; interaction involves physical manipulations objects au- dio/visual output. TagTrainer software system runs personal computer connected TagTiles board, used select, author, execute interactive exercises board. TagTrainer supports upper extremity rehabilitation neurological patients including stroke survivors, multiple sclerosis patients, spinal cord injury patients cerebral palsy patients. TagTrainer help train daily living skills, e.g., opening box, drinking cup, eating knife fork, etc., prompting patient carry relevant manipulations objects providing stimulating feedback. Typically, exercises consist multiple it- erations target area TagTiles board lights up, patient needs touch area appropriate side object (see Figure 2). Rather prepackaging exercises interactive board, first therapeutic applications TagTiles [10, 11], TagTrainer provides simple time- 6 line based programming interface (TagTrainer Exercise Creator, see Figure 3). Exercises modified created dragging actions (such as: ‘place object’, ‘move object’, etc.) onto timeline, assigning RFID-tagged parts object (e.g., bottom cup, index finger glove) actions, indi- cating corresponding target areas TagTiles board. This TagTrainer Exercise Creator interface allows therapists create exercises patient addressing specific training needs explicit requests. In therapy session supported TagTrainer therapist might commence inquir- ing patient wishes train, rapidly create exercise retrieve exer- cise created earlier, ask patient train certain duration num- ber repetitions. This type approach fundamentally changes role therapists; next role caregivers - instruct, monitor encourage - also responsible creating software content. Programming exercises TagTiles straightforward using TagTrainer, though creating interactive ap- plications without TagTrainer requires considerable effort software develop- ment expertise. Rogers [29] famously describes five-stage model diffusion innovations 19 provides us sufficient theoretical guidance propose suitable methods evaluation different stages EUD deployments. The five stages model (adapted ([29], p. 169)): 1. Knowledge, occurs individual exposed innovation’s exist- ence gains understanding functions. 2. Persuasion, occurs individual engages activities lead choice adopt reject innovation. 3. Decision, takes place individual engages activities lead choice adopt reject innovation. Implementation, occurs individual puts new idea use. 4. 5. Confirmation, takes place individual seeks reinforcement innovation-decision already made, may reverse previous decision exposed conflicting messages innovation. Importantly, five stages Rogers’ model show us different moments deployment process, different factors become important end user relation adoption technology deployed. If turn question started section - best way capture evi- dence successful EUD deployments - use Rogers’ model define stage evidence could collected support statement success EUD deployment: 1. Knowledge: evaluate end users’ understanding EUD system be- ing deployed e.g., usability functionality. 2. Persuasion: evaluate end users’ attitude towards system, exam- ple using UTAUT model [6] self-efficacy regarding EUD [15]. 4. 3. Decision: evaluate whether, opinion end users, adopting EUD practices lead positive outcome cost/benefit tradeoff related adoption EUD practices (relative advantage, Rogers’ theory [29]). Blackwell’s Attention Investment model [30, 31] could used gauge specifically EUD. Implementation: evaluate EUD practices end users develop, role EUD starts playing context deployed, importantly, extent EUD practices help end user achieve goals. 5. Confirmation: evaluate whether decision (not) engage EUD sustained period time, not, caused end user reverse initial decision. The advantage designing evaluations staged approach, outlined above, possible relate different evaluation studies pinpoint precisely areas improvements. It also protects us setting large, time-consuming expensive evaluations study EUD practices, earlier stage detect threads successful deployment (e.g., usability flaws, acceptance issues). The first two stages can, principle, even evaluated laboratory setting. Finally, structured staged evalua- tion approach allows us better compare different cases EUD deployments. Conclusion Evaluating EUD deployments far trivial, since difficult define precise subject evaluation determine approach methods suitable evaluation. One les- son draw survey evaluations EUD deployments far share common framework form rather fragmented picture. Developing Mashup Tools End-Users: On Importance Application Domain Florian Daniel, Muhammad Imran, Stefano Soi, Antonella De Angeli, Christopher R. Wilkinson, Fabio Casati Maurizio Marchese University Trento, Via Sommarive 5, 38123, Trento, Italy The recent emergence mashup tools refueled research end-user development, i.e., enabling end-users without programming skills compose applications. Yet, similar happened analogous promises web service composition business process management, research mostly focused technology and, consequence, failed objective. Plain technology (e.g., SOAP/WSDL web services) simple modeling languages (e.g., Yahoo! Pipes) don’t convey enough meaning non-programmers. In article, propose domain-speciﬁc approach mashups “speaks language user”, i.e., aware terminology, concepts, rules, conventions (the domain) user comfortable with. We show developing domain-speciﬁc mashup tool means, role mashup meta-model domain model play merged domain-speciﬁc mashup meta-model. We exemplify approach implementing mashup tool speciﬁc scenario (research evaluation) describe respective user study. The results ﬁrst user study conﬁrm domain-speciﬁc mashup tools indeed lower entry barrier mashup development. Keywords: Mashup tools, Mashup meta-model, Domain-speciﬁc mashup 1. INTRODUCTION Mashups typically simple web applications (most times consisting one single page) that, rather coded scratch, developed integrating reusing available data, functionalities, pieces user interfaces accessible Web. For instance, housingmaps.com integrates housing oﬀers Craigslist Google map, adding value two individual applications. Mashup tools, i.e., online development runtime environments mashups, ambitiously aim enabling non-programmers (regular web users) develop applications, some- times even situational applications developed ad hoc speciﬁc immediate need. Yet, similar happened web service composition, mashup platforms developed far tend expose much functionality many technicalities powerful ﬂexible suitable programmers. Alternatively, allow compositions simple little use practical applications. For example, mashup tools typically come SOAP services, RSS feeds, UI widgets, like. Non-programmers understand kinds compositional elements [Namoun et al. 2010a; 2010b]. We experienced mashup com- position platforms, mashArt [Daniel et al. 2009] MarcoFlow [Daniel et al. 2010], believe simpler usable many composition tools still failed suitable non-programmers [Mehandjiev et al. 2011]. Yet, amenable non-programmers increasingly important opportunity given wider wider range available on- line applications increased ﬂexibility required businesses personal life management raise need situational (one-use short-lifespan) applications cannot developed maintained traditional requirement elicitation software development processes. THE RESEVAL MASH TOOL FOR RESEARCH EVALUATION The methodology described result experience implementation domain-speciﬁc mashup platform, ResEval Mash2. ResEval Mash mashup platform research evaluation, i.e., assessment productivity quality researchers, teams, institutions, journals, like. The platform speciﬁcally tailored need sourcing data scientiﬁc publications researchers Web, aggregating them, computing metrics (also complex ad-hoc ones), visualizing them. ResEval Mash hosted mashup platform client-side editor runtime engine, running inside common web browser. It supports processing also large amounts data, feature achieved via sensible distribution respective computation steps client server. In following, show ResEval Mash implemented, starting domain models introduced throughout previous sections. 5.1 Design Principles Starting considerations Section 3, implementation ResEval Mash based set design principles (described next), think crucial success mashup platform like ResEval Mash. The ﬁrst last principles stem prior work user studies [Namoun et al. 2010b] context web service composition end- user development, second third principles respond domain-speciﬁc requirements identiﬁed analysis domain based past experience similar problems context LiquidPub European project3. (1) Hidden data mappings. In order prevent users deﬁning data mappings, mashup component used platform able understand manipulate domain concepts expressed DCM, deﬁnes domain entities relations. That is, accept input produce output domain entities (e.g., researchers, publications, metric values). Since components, hence, speak language, composition without explicit data mappings enough model component feeds input component. (2) Data-intensive processes. Although apparently simple, chosen domain peculiar may require processing large amounts data. For instance, may need extract process publications Italian researchers, i.e., average several dozens publications sixty-one thousand researchers (and Italian scenarios). Loading large amounts data remote services processing browser client side unfeasible due bandwidth, resource, time restrictions. Data processing therefore kept server side (we achieve via dedicated RESTful web services running mashup server). (3) Platform-speciﬁc services. As opposed common web services, typically designed independent external world, previous two principles instead demand services speciﬁcally designed implemented eﬃciently run domain- speciﬁc architecture. That is, must aware platform run in. As see, allows services access shared resources (e.g., data passed among components) protected speedy fashion. (4) Runtime transparency. Finally, research evaluation processes like reference scenario focus processing data, – mashup paradigm point view – demands data ﬂow mashup paradigm. Although data ﬂows relatively intuitive design time, typically intuitive runtime, especially processing data ﬂow logic takes several seconds (as could happen case). In order convey user going execution, therefore want provide transparency state running mashup. We identify two key points transparency important mashup model: compo- nent state processing state. At instant time execution mashup, runtime environment allow user inspect data processed produced component, environment graphically communicate processing progress animating graphical representation mashup model suitable colors. These principles require ResEval Mash speciﬁcally take account characteristics research evaluation domain. Doing produces platform fundamentally diﬀerent generic mashup platforms, Yahoo! As already discussed above, component implemented two diﬀerent ways: client-side JavaScript code (client component) server-side web service (server com- ponent). The choice client-side server-side implementation depends mainly type component created, may UI component (i.e., component user interact runtime graphical interface) service component (i.e., component runs speciﬁc business logic UI). UI components (e.g., Bar Chart scenario) always implemented client-side JavaScript ﬁles since must directly interact browser create manage graphical user interface. Service components (e.g., Microsoft Academic Publications scenario), instead, implemented ways, depending characteristics. In research evaluation domain, since typically deal large amounts data, service components commonly imple- mented server-side web services. In way, computational power constraints present client-side and, moreover, exploit platform features oﬀered server-side, like Shared Memory mechanism, which, e.g., permit eﬃciently deal data-intensive processes. In cases, particular computa- tional requirements, service component implemented via client-side JavaScript, runs directly browser. The JavaScript implementation, case UI service components, must include functions implementing component’s business logic. For example, Italian Researchers service component implemented server-side since deal large amounts data (i.e., thousands researchers), belongs server components category (introduced Section 5.3). This type components, correctly work within domain-speciﬁc platform, must implemented Java RESTful web service following speciﬁc implementation guidelines. In particular, service must able properly communicate parts system and, thus, must aware data passing patterns discussed shared memory. RELATED WORK Although requirement intuitive development environments design support end-users clearly emerges research end-user development (EUD), example web services [Namoun et al. 2010a; 2010b], little available satisfy need. There currently two main approaches enable less skilled users develop programs: general, development eased either simplifying (e.g., limiting expressive power programming lan- guage) reusing knowledge (e.g., copying pasting existing algorithms). Among simpliﬁcation approaches, workﬂow Business Process Management (BPM) community one ﬁrst propose abstraction business processes tasks control ﬂows would allow also less skilled users deﬁne processes. Yet, according opinion, approach achieved little success modeling still requires training knowledge. The advent service-oriented architecture (SOA) substituted tasks services, yet com- position still challenging task even expert developers [Namoun et al. 2010a; 2010b]. The reuse approach implemented program libraries, services, templates (such generics Java process templates workﬂows). It provides building blocks composed achieve goal, entire composition (the algorithm - possibly made generic templates used), may may suit developer’s needs. Mashups aim bring together beneﬁts simpliﬁcation reuse. In case domain-speciﬁc mashup environments, aim push simpliﬁcation even compared generic mashup platforms limiting environment (and, hence, expressive power) needs single, well-deﬁned domain only. Reuse supported form reusable domain activities, mashed up. As such, work presented paper related three key areas, i.e., domain-speciﬁc modeling, web service composition, mashups, brieﬂy overview following. Domain-speciﬁc modeling. The idea focusing particular domain exploiting speciﬁcities create eﬀective simpler development environments supported large number research works [L´edeczi et al. 2001] [Costabile et al. 2004] [Mernik et al. 2005] [France Rumpe 2005]. Mainly areas related Domain Speciﬁc Modeling (DSM) Domain Speciﬁc Language (DSL). In DSM, domain concepts, rules, semantics represented one models, translated executable code. Managing models complex task typically suited programmers that, however, increases his/her productivity. This possible thanks provision domain-speciﬁc programming instruments abstract low-level programming details powerful code generators “implement” behalf modeler. Studies using diﬀerent DSM tools (e.g., commercial MetaEdit+ tool academic solution MIC [L´edeczi et al. 2001]) shown developers’ productivity increased order magnitude. In DSL context, although ﬁnd solutions targeting end-users (e.g., Excel macros) medium skilled users (e.g., MatLab), current DSLs target expert developers (e.g., Swashup [Maximilien et al. 2007]). Also introduction “domain” raises abstraction level, typical textual nature languages makes less intuitive harder manage less suitable end-users compared visual approaches. Beneﬁts limits DSM DSL approaches summarized [France Rumpe 2005] [Mernik et al. 2005]. Web service composition. Ontology-based Interpretation Keywords Semantic Search Thanh Tran, Philipp Cimiano, Sebastian Rudolph Rudi Studer Institute AIFB, Universität Karlsruhe, Germany {dtr,pci,sru,rst}@aifb.uni-karlsruhe.de Abstract. Current information retrieval (IR) approaches formally capture explicit meaning keyword query provide comfortable way user specify information needs basis keywords. Ontology-based ap- proaches allow sophisticated semantic search impose query syntax diﬃcult handle. In paper, present approach translating keyword queries DL conjunctive queries using background knowledge available on- tologies. We present implementation shows interpretation keywords used exploration asserted knowledge semantics-based declarative query answering process. We also present evalua- tion system discussion limitations approach respect underlying assumptions directly points issues future work. 1 Introduction Part Semantic Web vision provide web-scale access semantically de- scribed content. In particular, implies understanding users’ information needs ac- curately enough allow retrieving precise answer using semantic technologies. Currently, web search engines however based purely statistical techniques. We present generic approach translating user question formal system query. 3.1 Models Ontology-based Information Retrieval In line models classical IR, namely query resource model [1], discuss four diﬀerent models involved ontology-based IR. The Mental Model OU: The mental model OU corresponds information need user mind beginning IR task. This model result user translating elements OU elements PU. Moreover (depending expressive means LU), might also ele- ments QU explicitly denoting gaps (like, e.g. question words natural language). S query language L0 Independent concrete formal language used, elements conceived set entities given ontology. As opposed abstract mental model OU, entities structure OS explicitly given directly accessible. These elements constitute knowledge (the KB) system uses answer user question. More precisely, means thought entities mental model OU correspond SHOIN(D) ontology entities disjoint union sets I (individuals), J (data values), C (concepts), D (data ranges), R (object properties), U (data properties) associations OU correspond associative interconnections types hi, Ci, hi1, R, i2i hi, U, ji i, i1, i2 ∈ I, j ∈ J, C ∈ C, R ∈ R, U ∈ U. In follows describe various steps concrete instantiation generic approach details. 4.1 Step One — Mapping Terms KB Entities Due A1’, assume keywords mapped ontology entities, namely indi- viduals, data values, concepts, data ranges, well object data properties. In particular, mapping deﬁned function f maps elements user question model QU entities system resource model OS , i.e. f : QU → OS . For practical purposes, crucial function “robust" sense also considers syntactic spelling variants. Using query engine, entities KB retrieved via URIs. In par- ticular, f implemented retrieval operation performed engine, e.g. simply passing URI input repository API. In order cope syn- tactic spelling variants, Lucene1 actually used index search engine. That is, URIs labels entities indexed, using fuzzy search feature Lucene, query generated entered keyword. The engine returns ontology entities ranked according syntactic similarity respective keyword. As one minor syntactic diﬀerence example last section, highest ranked entities QU ="Philipp Cimiano X-Media publications" indeed Philipp Cimiano, X-Media publication. The request sent e-mail 12 people responded. Some queries obviously scope knowledge base removed, resulting total 42 diﬀerent queries. These queries incorporated evaluation set used development tuning approach. Examples queries diﬀerent number keywords posed users are: "projects Blohm" (Retrieve projects Sebastian Blohm working on), "phone Rudi Studer" (Retrieve phone number Rudi Studer) "publications SmartWeb Pascal Hitzler 2002" (Retrieve publications published Pascal Hitzler within SmartWeb 2002). For evalua- tion, one authors manually assigned conjunctive queries according natural language description. A query generated approach regarded correct retrieved answers hand crafted query. In line work question an- swering ([5],[4]), evaluate approach terms precision, recall F-Measure. Precision P deﬁned number correctly translated keyword queries (based equivalence results) divided number cases system able construct query. Recall R deﬁned number correctly translated keyword queries divided keyword queries evaluation set, i.e. 42 case. The F1 = 2∗P∗R P+R measure harmonic mean precision recall. In case query selected hand diﬀerent queries generated, sys- tem obtains precision P = 85%, recall R = 52% F-Measure F1 = 64%. In case automatically choose highest ranked question instead, results slightly lower precision P = 69%, recall R = 43% F-Measure F1 = 53%. 5.3 Discussion Our evaluation performed knowledge base institute portal automatically generated3. The underlying ontology SWRC ontology [11], allows representation researchers, publications, active projects etc. 3 see http://www.aifb.uni-karlsruhe.de/ The evaluation carried involving colleagues, visit update portal pages frequently, know underlying ontology detail. Thus, queries users experiments asked system contain keywords correspond entities knowledge base. Obviously, problem approach violates assumptions A1’ well generic A1. According recall measure (43-52%), least half keyword queries fulﬁll assumption A1’ thus mapped appropriate ontology elements. The higher precision 69-85% hand shows that, given A1’ fulﬁlled, generated query correct cases. In fact, found errors approach produced step 1. This means Lucene engine return appropriate ontology elements cases. This problem could example addressed integrating additional lexical knowledge words found resources WordNet [12]. A issue related assumption A2, i.e. assumption on- tology entities keywords map connected via paths given length d. We experimented length 3 approach. Possibly, higher re- call could achieved using higher value d, also probable much "background noise" would introduced, thus making selection rele- vant query diﬃcult. Overall, assumptions proved valuable. Our ﬁrst assumption (A1 / A1’) states users conceptualize information need terms compatible underlying ontology. While assumption quite simplistic one hand rather strict other, turned necessary questions fulﬁll assumption anyway conceptual range system. From practical point view, assumption thus necessary. As- sumption A2, assumes ontology elements connected paths maximal length turned crucial order restrict search space speciﬁc part KB. 6 Conclusions We presented generic approach mapping queries user language expressive logical language. In particular, presented particular instantiation generic approach translates keyword queries DL conjunctive queries using knowledge available KB. We clariﬁed particular assumptions approach based on. We presented current implementation system well ﬁrst results evaluation translation process. The evaluation shows promising results w.r.t. precision, still lower recall deﬁnitely increased integrating lexical knowledge process matching keywords ontology elements. In light evaluation results, argued assumptions indeed reasonable necessary interpretation answering queries using ontologies. Besides integration lexical knowledge improve recall, intend also improve runtime performance approach. focus future work boosting performance. So far, process interpretation relies mainly assertional knowledge, resulting large number A-Box queries need processed exploration. We plan exploit available T-Box knowledge “guided exploration" connections ontology entities reduce number A- Box queries. One major problem approach suﬀers fact consider keywords ambiguous respect labels ontology simply con- siders ﬁrst matching ontology element start exploration. Currently, case ambiguities, exploration would performed possible inter- pretations query term. However, alternatives explored might exponential number possible interpretations keywords. Future work thus aim appropriate treatment ambiguities. Finally, develop presented system support integrated ap- proach combined search exploration knowledge bases. [32, 31]), mainly based system interactions, rather visual formalism, generates queries target formal textual form. A VQS may may use VQL [61]; not, system likely express subset underlying language. Such descriptions typically composed select, join projection functions (cf. [59]). The select op- eration meant specifying conditions concepts mergers ﬁltering result set (e.g., clause SQL), project operation determines attributes displayed (i.e., se- lect clause SQL). [89]). Although ontology-based approaches introduce natural way joining concepts, still issues, discussed below. A fundamental distinction queries depends query conditions connected considered twofold: conjunctive disjunctive queries. The former basically realised merging query conditions logical “AND” connectives (cf. [40,71]), latter formed logical “OR” connectives (cf. [45]). Queries seen several topological categories, linear queries, queries branching cyclic queries. The ﬁrst refers queries basically serial joins concepts, second category queries composed path expressions branching (cf. [7]). The latter refers queries least path (undirected) query graph whose ﬁrst node corresponds last. An example cyclic query would “ﬁnd persons work city born” (“bornIn” “worksIn” relationships, “person” “city” concepts). Queries including disjunction cycles problematic compared linear tree-shaped conjunctive queries cycles usually addressed node duplication approach (cf. [36,93]) – i.e., duplicating city node previous example. There query types cannot purely ﬁt aforementioned topological forms, queries quantiﬁcation, negation, aggregation. Two fundamen- tal forms quantiﬁcation existential quantiﬁcation universal quantiﬁcation (cf. [126,95]). Existential quantiﬁcation interpreted “there exists”, “there exists least one”, “for some”, OWL “some values from” (cf. [10,166]); example query would “ﬁnd lecturers teach least one math course”. Universal quantiﬁcation interpreted “for all”, “for every”, “only” and, OWL “all values from” (cf. [166]); example query would “ﬁnd lecturers teach Introduction Computing course”. Negation operation may applied proposition, truth- value etc., simplest terms, used reverse condition; example query would “ﬁnd students take Introduction Computing course” (e.g., [10]). Aggregation functions count, sum max used group values multiple attributes form single value. While use existential quan- tiﬁcation remains implicit, queries include universal quantiﬁcation, negation, aggregation quite es- oteric end users; even applies skilled users, particularly universal quantiﬁers (cf. [95]). Finally, query manipulation query reformulation often expected situations. However, query manipu- lation, opposed query reformulation scratch, needs special attention. ABSTRACT Eliciting user-task models thorny problem model- based user interface design, communicating domain- specific knowledge expert knowledge engineer continuing problem knowledge acquisition. We devised task elicitation method capitalizes domain expert’s ability describe task plain English, knowledge engineer’s skills formalize it. The method bridges gap two helping expert refine description giving engineer clues structure. We implemented evaluated interactive tool called User-Task Elicitation Tool (U-TEL) elicit user-task models domain experts based methodology. Via direct manipulation, U-TEL provides capabilities word processing, keyword classification, outline refinement, By using U-TEL, domain experts refine textual specification user task basic user-task interface suitable model-based model use development environments. Our evaluation shows U-TEL used effectively domain experts without background programming interface modeling, tool key element promoting user-centered interface design model-based systems. Tailorability emphasized building tech- nology architecture support user-driven modiﬁcation design process, ultimately throughout life system. Three activities characterize modiﬁable software identiﬁed: (1) choosing alternative anticipated designed-for behaviors, (2) constructing new behaviors existing pieces smaller parts, (3) altering artifact (the radical sort change) [1]. Early research drew distinction adaptable adaptive systems; former ﬂexible use may change behavior parts, latter dynamically adapting user’s needs [2]. Signiﬁcant difﬁculties inherent proposed approaches, however, including problems ranging need increased development efforts potential unwillingness end users contribute system designers. The challenge creating modiﬁable software beginning realized systems emerging EUD-related research 1980s 1990s. For example, Trigg et al. 1987 explored adapting tailoring hypertext system [3]; Fischer proposed Seeding, Evolutionary Growth, Reseeding (SER) Model wherein system “seed” co-designed, allowed grow naturally use, re-seeded reorganize necessary [4], well constructed knowledge-based design environ- ments (DODEs) provided constantly evolving space users shaped system [5]. During time, signiﬁcant changes technology approach taken studying design, construction, use. Notable researchers human-computer interaction (HCI) identiﬁed “waves” change driving need reassess focus design activities context use tools evolved (summarized Table 1, below). Academic Editor: Emilio Ferrara Received: 20 January 2016; Accepted: 8 March 2016; Published: 29 March 2016 Abstract: Every day, active social network user produces shares texts, images videos. While developers access data application programming interfaces (APIs) creating games, visualizations routines, end users less control information. Their access mediated social application features, limits combining sources, ﬁltering results performing actions groups elements. In order ﬁll gap, introduce FaceMashup, end user development (EUD) environment supporting manipulation Facebook graph. We describe tool interface, documenting choices made design iterations. Data types represented widgets containing user interface (UI) elements similar used social network application. Widgets connected drag drop inner ﬁelds, application updates content. Finally, report results user-test FaceMashup prototype, shows good acceptance environment end-users. Introduction The availability application programming interfaces (APIs) accessing social network data gold-mine developers, since provide standard, easy trustworthy way requesting user’s information, useful personalized application experience. Social Network APIs, Facebook Graph API [1], apply strict rules order protect users’ privacy. They must explicitly grant access speciﬁc information different applications. For instance, simple application show contents created friends restaurant, taking location input. Provided application permission access data, costs lines code developers retrieve contents created user’s friends speciﬁc place, combining comments, photos videos. In contrast, even users owners data, less control it. The social network application mediates access: cannot combine sources, ﬁlter results perform speciﬁc actions explicitly provided interface. Considering increasing growth social networks’ size, users already overwhelmed contents created every day [2]. This opened market third party applications provide visualizations speciﬁc user interests (e.g., travelling, restaurants, music etc.). However, also difﬁcult third party developers support Future Internet 2016, 8, 10; doi:10.3390/ﬁ8020010 www.mdpi.com/journal/futureinternet future internet Future Internet 2016, 8, 10 2 17 unusual searches speciﬁc tasks smaller potential audience. Nevertheless, small groups, functionalities useful. In paper, propose ﬁll gap applying End User Development (EUD) techniques enabling end-users inspect control social network data, creating applications able retrieve manipulate contents. We describe design iterations implementation FaceMashup, environment allowing users mix contents contents created friends, perform actions them. As example social network, focus Facebook. FaceMashup represents Graph API data (e.g., user proﬁles, photos, comments, videos, etc.) elements used social network application interface (e.g., Facebook web client case), including dedicated widgets. Starting building blocks, user drag interface elements different widgets retrieving, combining ﬁltering data In addition, (s)he perform actions selected entries coming Facebook graph. set action widgets. In last part paper, report preliminary user study FaceMashup prototype, shows good completion rate, even difﬁcult tasks (e.g., combination ﬁltering two data sources), good overall acceptance tool proposed EUD techniques, together need effective help widget connection. 2. End User Development Support Considering problems elicited ﬁrst prototype, redesigned social network data representation actions deﬁning routine ﬂow. We summarize design principles followed creating environment user interface (UI), motivated choices. P1 No separation design runtime SNA. The user deﬁnes data application logic executing SNA. The goal require lower abstraction effort end user, since (s)he focuses speciﬁc example, generalization demanded underlying tool support, happens programming example paradigm [7]. P2 A data type represented interface elements exploited social network application UI. Since user familiar data presentation, exploit recognition mechanism helping user understanding semantics data type (e.g., user proﬁle represented proﬁle picture, name etc.). If data type contains inner ﬁelds, representation allows us provide access inner elements (e.g., name user proﬁle, place photo, etc.). In case, follow approach [5], data UI element end user’s point view. P3 The user exploits familiar interactions deﬁning input routine element exploiting output. He consider UI objects manipulated inside environment like e.g., ﬁle icons. P4 The environment support user guiding his/her actions creating application logic, order (1) avoid conceptual errors (e.g., data type mismatch) (2) explore possibilities offered environment stimulating learning process. The combination P3 P4 supports users gradual transition ﬁrst intuitive manipulation data action representation towards reﬂective stage understanding concepts using scaffolding approach [12]. As happens environments exploit similar widget representation [4,5], connection mechanism deﬁnes data dependency temporal relationship among widgets included environment. The convention adopted FaceMashup similar data-ﬂow based approaches EUD (e.g., Yahoo Pipes): time data source widget changes value, connected widgets update values accordingly. Therefore, connection deﬁnes implicitly partial temporal ordering among widgets, automatically computed underling environment support. FaceMashup represents explicitly ordering positioning widgets inside environment. It represents sequential relationship widget A B positioning B A vertical axis. Instead, user may provide information needed widgets time (theoretically) order (more practically), widgets represented position vertical axis, aligned horizontally. This creates grid widgets, rows represent sequential steps column parallel operations. The environment maintains widget ordering follows. It positions new widget ﬁrst row. Environment Implementation In section, provide technical details environment implementation We open share source code groups research. Please contact corresponding author additional information. Figure 10 shows overall architecture FaceMashup. The tool consists client-side part responsible supporting user interaction, server side part maintains application state. The initial state client side front-end generated JavaServer Page (JSP). During interaction user, environment creates modiﬁes elements Javascript code, communicating asynchronously server jQuery. The server-side part consists Java Servlet exposes functionalities REST (Representational State Transfer) API. In addition, server communicates Facebook order store retrieve social Future Internet 2016, 8, 10 13 17 network data Graph API [1]. The client-side part never invokes directly Facebook API, allowing server persist user actions. Figure 10. FaceMashup architecture In order retrieve data Facebook API, FaceMashup environment registered speciﬁc Facebook application. Therefore, set speciﬁc read write permissions user’s data, controlled Facebook user privacy settings. The environment maintains two different descriptions widgets. The ﬁrst related widget type, contains speciﬁcation input output data provided supporting connection suggestions. The matching criteria hard coded environment, inferred widget description. This allows easily adding new types widgets. The second widget description related instances currently included SNA. Such description includes widget identiﬁer, list incoming connections, ﬁlter deﬁnition, information widget internal state (e.g., currently displayed item photo list etc.). An application represented server-side graph widget descriptions. The update process SNA starts notiﬁcation client user’s action, e.g., new connection. The server receives notiﬁcation REST API updates involved graph node representation accordingly, querying Facebook graph necessary. After that, adds elements node forward star update queue, repeats operation queue empty. At end process, server sends back client JSON (JavaScript Object Notation) formatted description state widgets need updated. A Javascript routine concludes client-side state synchronization. The widget graph description allows serialization de-serialization application state, enabling loading saving SNAs sharing future reuse. 7.
