End-User-Oriented Telco Mashups: The OMELETTE ABSTRACT With success Web 2.0 witnessing growing number services APIs exposed Telecom, IT content providers. Targeting Web community and, particular, Web application developers, service providers ex- pose capabilities infrastructures applications order open new markets reach new customer groups. However, due complexity underly- ing technologies, last step, i.e., consumption integration oﬀered services, non-trivial time- consuming task still prerogative expert develop- ers. Although many approaches lower entry barriers end users exist, little success achieved far. In paper, introduce OMELETTE1 project show addresses end-user-oriented telco mashup de- velopment. We present goals project, describe contributions, summarize current results, describe current future work. Categories Subject Descriptors H.4.3 [Information Systems Applications]: Communi- cations Applications; D.2.11 [Software Engineering]: Soft- ware Architectures—Service-oriented architecture (SOA); C.2.4 [Distributed Systems]: Distributed applications General Terms Theory Keywords Mashup, Telco Services, EUD 1Open Mashup Enterprise service platform LinkEd data The TElco domain Copyright held International World Wide Web Conference Com- mittee (IW3C2). Distribution papers limited classroom use, personal use others. WWW 2012 Companion, April 16–20, 2012, Lyon, France. ACM 978-1-4503-1230-1/12/04. 1. INTRODUCTION Over last years, large number novel telephony messaging services become available Web community. These so-called telco services provide unique ca- pabilities regarding voice, video data transfer [5]. From usage point view, telco services invoked using provider-speciﬁc Web APIs shield developer complexity protocol mismatch operator networks. Although several initiatives aim standardize cross- provider service interfaces2 exist, integration telco ser- vices Web applications still challenging. First, data streaming, real time communication conferencing still lack suitable technologies integration techniques. Sec- ond, without appropriate tools platforms, deploy- ment, management execution converged services re- mains complex costly task. Finally, current state- of-the-art platforms support end users adequately, users hindered building solutions without speciﬁc programming skills. To meet challenges FP7 project OMELETTE speciﬁcally focuses needs telco domain end user development. Based technologies lessons learned Web mashups, project investigates in- novative ways letting end users create collabo- ration platforms. In particular, OMELETTE contributes state art follows: 1. OMELETTE provides set tools components support development execution telco mashups. OMELETTE introduces open extensible architec- ture based several open-source projects. Special at- tention goes interoperability among mashups envi- ronments, achieved standardizing mod- els produced artifacts. 2. OMELETTE assists end-users developers development task means recommendation automatic composition engine. The recommenda- tion engine able interactively provide help manual development; automated composition en- gine able take development work. 2http://oneapi.gsmworld.com/ WWW 2012 – European Projects Track April 16–20, 2012, Lyon, France235 Both instruments guide developers/end users, allowing expand development capabilities develop faster. 3. OMELETTE contributes several open source projects (Apache Wookie Apache Rave) well emerging W3C standards (W3C Widgets WebID). Apache Rave extended capability discover widgets remote sources, share workspace models environments, deliver development recom- mendations, compose workspaces user goals. Apache Wookie support extended widget logic, particular, inter-widget communication. 2. MOTIVATING SCENARIO To motivate research telco service integration dedicated mashup tools, consider following scenario. Mike working small company Portugal pro- duces wooden toys kids. He responsible launch new product distributed via large chain stores Europe. To discuss latest orders stores decide possible discount, Mike needs or- ganize meeting among him, representative chain located Paris colleague sales de- partment currently business trip. To hold live meeting among geographically distributed participants, Mike would like adaptable web-platform pro- vides conﬁgurable access data existing enterprise systems (e.g. CRM, ERP) seamless integration collaboration communication functionalities. He wants create view data required next meet- ing share others. Before making decisions, Mike asks colleagues provide feedback. As such, limited Internet access available, participants use communication channels, telco operator net- work. The internal web portal Mike’s company seems promising candidate become platform. It pro- vides dashboard widgets integrated catalog. However, capabilities portal limited terms extensibility communication support. In addi- tion, existing widgets provide generic data cannot combined connected. The presented scenario reveals following challenges: • Native integration telco capabilities existing web applications portals avoid extra software solutions minimize distraction switching tools contexts. • Easy extensibility inter-widget communication creating collaboration spaces widgets. • Lower barrier end-users create integrate widgets, i.e. creating mashups serve users’ needs. 3. THE OMELETTE APPROACH Instead proposing yet another mashup editor similar Yahoo! Pipes, idea behind OMELETTE approach leverage much possible existing mashup environ- ments. The goal come integrated environ- ment able provide role mashup devel- opment life cycle right tool matching role’s in- dividual skills. The idea line spirit mashups themselves, i.e., reuse, aims leveraging previous experiences results, also stemming projects. OMELETTE creates architecture based open stan- dards interfaces, components replaced integrated seamless way. Figure 1 gives overview OMELETTE toolkit shows end-users interact diﬀerent mashup environments. 3.1 The Live Environment End users provided composition environment widgets (the so-called live environment), placed canvas, synchronized among other, shared users. Widgets provide user interface complex application logic, aggregated data services telco functionality. The composition paradigm low complex- ity seen fusion current portal mashup technologies. The key innovation live environ- ment - also gives name - capability sup- port ”live” composition approach, widgets, placed composition area (the so-called workspace), immediately executed, users inspect interact them. Furthermore, live environment en- ables widgets communicate other, providing added value otherwise static set widgets [7]. This fundamentally diﬀerent current practice, composition typically based explicit composition mod- els [3]. Instead manipulating abstract modeling constructs (which relatively intuitive experts, may meaning end users) representing software artifacts, live environment allows end users directly manipulate software artifacts (the widgets). The Live Environment based Apache Rave Apache Wookie projects makes use open Web standards (HTML5, advanced CSS features, W3C Widgets, Device APIs). The Live En- vironment runs regular mobile web browsers, making use available device capabilities. Apache Rave provides lightweight Java platform host, serve ag- gregate widgets (OpenSocial Gadgets W3C Widgets). It manages workspace instances orchestrates background services build complete workspace given user. OMELETTE extends Apache Rave inter-widget choreography service, dynamic loader interfaces to- ward recommendation automatic composition en- gines. Apache Wookie acts widget repository, widgets published available Live Environment. It manages widget instances supports inter-widget choreography providing additional messag- ing facilities. 3.2 Recommendation Automatic Compo- sition Engine OMELETTE recognizes enough simplify technology order enable end users develop mashups. End users (and partly also developers) need assisted development task, either yet acquainted mashup environment simply able ﬁnd good solution (e.g., right components right combination thereof). OMELETTE therefore equips mashup environments two novel features: recommendation engine able interactively provide end users developers help manual development, automated com- position engine able take develop- ment work (Figure 1). The knowledge base used compute ranked contextualized list composition patterns aimed helping user complete his/her workspace devel- opment. Retrieved patterns shown end users dedicated recommendation panel next workspace. Once user selected recommendation, chosen pattern weaved current workspace model, i.e., connected existing workspace model. The automatic composition engine allows users focus (the goal) mashup supposed instead (in terms implementation). In following highlight possible classiﬁcations languages, become handy EUD. In particular, describe dimensions focus, style notation. The focus DSL either vertical horizontal. Vertical DSLs aim speciﬁc industry ﬁeld. Examples vertical DSLs may include: conﬁguration languages home automation systems, modeling languages biological experiments, analysis languages ﬁnancial applications, on. On side, 62 End-user Development & Mashups: State Art horizontal DSLs broader applicability technical broad nature allows concepts apply across large group applications. Examples horizontal DSLs include SQL, Flex , WebML , many others. The style DSL either declarative imperative. Declarative DSLs adopt speciﬁcation paradigm expresses logic computation without describing control ﬂow. In words, language deﬁnes program accomplish, rather describing accomplish it. Im- perative DSLs instead speciﬁcally require deﬁning executable algorithm states steps control ﬂow needs followed successfully complete job. The notation DSL either graphical textual. The graphical DSLs (also known Domain Speciﬁc Modeling Lan- guages, DSML) imply outcomes development visual models development primitives graphical items blocks, arrows edges, containers, symbols, on. The textual DSLs comprise several categories, including XML- based notations, structured text notations, textual conﬁguration ﬁles, on. China {wangguiling, yhan}@software.ict.ac.cn ABSTRACT This paper presents end-user-oriented programming environment called Mashroom. Major contributions herein include end-user programming model expressive data structure well set formally-defined mashup operators. The data structure takes advantage nested table, maintains intuitiveness allowing users express complex data objects. The mashup operators visualized contextual menu formula bar directly applied data. Experiments case studies reveal end users little difficulty effectively efficiently using Mashroom build mashup applications. For definition nested relational model, please refer paper [16]. In nested relational model, data represented nested tables. Figure 3 shows example nested table. The “Theaters” relation specifies list theaters located certain region. It two atomic attributes (“Theater name” “Address”) sub-relation named “Movies”. The sub-relation “Movies” describes movies show theater. Nested table offers simple intuitive access underlying data sources. For example above, data related movies represented directly sub-relation “Theaters”, rather distributing another distinct table. Thanks recursive algebra foundation query language nested table [16], developers access manipulate sub-relation data directly, without restructure un-nested relation. For example, recursive selectionσ(Theaters(MoviesLabel=”Waiting Beijing”)) used directly access theaters certain movie show. In Mashroom, underlying data mashup application also called MashSheet, consists set worksheets. A worksheet fact nested table, column represents atom attribute sub-relation, row represents tuple. An atom attribute worksheet one six types: text, textlink, img, imglink, video, videolink. Different traditional spreadsheet “cell” first- class object, MashSheet takes “column” first-class objects. Technology: ease use integration task support complexity criticality changeability reduce reliability accuracy security risk Meta-design Meta-design characterizes objectives, techniques, processes creating new media environ- ments allowing “owners problems” (that is, end users) act designers. A fundamental objective meta-design create socio-technical environ- ments empower users engage actively continuous development systems rather restricted use existing systems. In design processes, two basic stages dif- ferentiated: design time use time. At design time, system developers (with without user involve- ment) create environments tools. In conven- tional design create complete systems. Because needs, objectives, situational contexts users anticipated design time, users often find system unfit tasks use time, thus requiring modification existing systems. To accommodate unexpected issues use time, systems must “underdesigned” design time. Introduction With proliferation Web APIs (i.e., reusable soft- ware components published Web), Web [1] become highly programmable platform. This due fact dominant type mashup users various application domains little knowledge programming related technologies. In order cope challenge, therefore, non- professional users need empowered create mashups. End-User Development (EUD) [9,10] research area committed address type problems. In paper, present detail design evaluation innovative mashup tool called Natural- Mash. NaturalMash provides adequate expressive power create non-trivial, feature-rich, interactive mashups composition Web APIs provided different technologies (ranging REST SOAP ser- vices JavaScript HTML5 widgets). This paper also contributes novel, hybrid end-user programming technique [13] based natural language programming [14], live programming, WYSIWYG [15] (What You See Is What You Get), Programming Demonstration [16] (PbD). Design goals, requirements, decisions One main challenges designing mashup tools consists balancing trade-off tool expressive power assumed user skills [21]. In addressing challenge, design NaturalMash seeks empower non-professional users (i.e., know programming) rapidly create useful feature- rich mashups minimal prior learning. To achieve this, tailored design meet three requirements: (i) high degree usability non-professional users (R1), (ii) competitive level expressive power (R2), (iii) usefulness sense able create useful mashups (R3). Abstract mashup components The underlying implementation CNL accommo- dates abstract component model (i) gives unified technology-neutral description Web APIs, (ii) models abstract textual form. Listing 3 builds mashup combining Twitter (https://dev. twitter.com/), YouTube player, HTML table, regular The NaturalMash component model classifies describes various aspects Web APIs. Different Web APIs broadly categorized three types: Data Source, delivers snapshot stream data remote sources Web (e.g., BBC News), Service, provides remotely accessible business logic (e.g., Yahoo! Place- maker), Widget, stand-alone Web application self-contained (reusable) user interface (e.g., Google Maps widget). The component model also distinguishes two types functionality provided Web APIs, namely, Task – passive atomic operation, Event – active source control. More detail, Event describes condition (if satisfied) may produce message. In Listing 1 example, selecting item (slide) Event table widget. A Task, hand, takes input data, processing, produces data. Finding Fig. 2. The grammar NaturalMash CNL represented Extended Backus–Naur Form (EBNF). 418 S. Aghaee, C. Pautasso / Journal Visual Languages Computing 25 (2014) 414–432 Fig. 3. The data consumed produced Tasks Events Web APIs modeled as, respectively, one input output parameters. Each parameter meaningful unique (only within API scope) name; syntactic semantic types defined shown end- users. To give natural language representation APIs, Tasks Events annotated specific natural language description called label. For instance, “find slides [keyword]” label describing slide- searching Task Slideshare API. The input parameter name keyword enclosed within square brackets, creating placeholder object verb used label. In mashup recipe, actual object sentence may parameter name referring output previous tasks, constant value, anaphora — linguistic, anaphora (e. g., “that”) defined expression linking two elements document — pointing specific part recipe text (e. g., Listing 3 clause “play it” contains anaphora “it” pointing output parameter event produced table Widget). CNL grammar Fig. 2 illustrates grammar NaturalMash CNL. The top-level structure mashup recipe text decom- posable paragraphs, are, turn, collection sentences. To describe compose together Web APIs, CNL imposes specific grammatical constraints, limit types sentences may constructed. We distinguish  Imperative sentences: They composed multiple imperative mood clauses. Each clause built Task label replacing placeholders label objects. For example, given label “find songs titled [keyword]” corresponding clause “find songs titled mashup”, object replaced constant value “mashup”.  Causal sentences: They written causal form time conjunction “when” introduces pas- sive clause (a label associated Event) followed set imperative mood clauses (like imperative sentence). The passive clause describes cause event; imperative mood clauses represent effect realized cause event happens. Consider causal sentence example (Listing 1). “When item selected, search youtube videos title.” In sentence, Event described “an item selected” happens, Task following “search youtube videos title” executed. The CNL compiled executable language imperative event-driven semantics. Its imperative aspect natural sequential order sentences English (i.e., left right) defines control flow mashup one sentence, clause, phrase another. The event-driven execution semantics Natural- Mash CNL modeled causal sentences. Causal state- ments activated (but immediately executed) main control flow reaches them. An activated causal sentence ready later receive control whenever Event happens, control immediately passed imperative part. In so, imperative part initiates parallel independent control flow, repeatedly executed every occurrence Event. 4. Architecture NaturalMash designed live mashup tool, completely automates repetitive task compiling, deploying, running mashup recipes. Considering mashups compositions remote distributed Web APIs, rather technically challenging comply requirements liveness, changes made user mashup design must reflected result mashup execution minimal delay. We present client/server architecture Natural- Mash (Fig. 5), aims achieving performance improvements result decreased response time elapsing instants user manipulates mashup created instant resulting mashup compiled, executed, displayed user. The client-side runs Web browser presents user interface mashup tool resulting mashup (in visual field). The server-side handles compila- tion recipes executable representations supports runtime, involves interactions external Web services Web data sources. To realize APIs affected user interactions intended live development, consider following scenario. The life-cycle interaction begins user making single modification model mashup developed client. This modification target either visual field (modify- ing user interface) textual field (editing recipe). Each modification may result server (re) compiling, (re)deploying, (re)executing, (re)rendering target mashup. Incremental change detector mashup models Mashup compilation deployment time- consuming tasks. Therefore, initially, soon user makes modification mashup, change detector component (client-side) identifies whether modification requires issuing (re)compilation request. The mechanism behind component based classifying possible modifications follows:  Front-end modifications applied mashup user interface (e.g., reorganizing widgets within mashup user interface layout, adding removing widgets), therefore, handled client-side with- need recompiling redeploying whole mashup. These modifications temporarily stored previewed, user0s session finished idle (to avoid losing modifications case disconnection), modifications sent server persistent storage.  Incomplete modifications require modifica- tions take visible effect. These may leave mashup recipe temporarily incorrect state user needs complete executed. For instance, new Task added, since data flow source bound input yet possible display results suitable Widget. These modifications may trigger display autocompletion menu highlight- ing errors partial compilation required.  Logic modifications change back-end mashup, thus require recompile, redeploy re-execute mashup server-side. Once change detector decides recompilation required, request sent server (over WebSockets). To facilitate speed compilation process, architecture includes change modeler component supports notion incremental compilation [27]. More detail, source model target model required generate change model. The source target models are, respectively, high-level (abstract) low-level (code) models mashup. The change model conforms metamodel defines possible changes may occur mashup (i.e., source target models) contains information link changes source model target model. Since many cases mashups grown incrementally adding one API time, possible extend low-level code without regenerate scratch. 5.2. Compilation deployment The resulting change model passed compiler component, responsible (i) generate executable code corresponding change model, (ii) merge existing code, finally (iii) redeploy mashup. This component allow terminate unfinished compilation process avoid continuous compilation requests short-intervals put heavy load server. The compiler component NaturalMash provides pipeline transforms mashup recipes executable models Web service compositions executed JOpera engine [28]. Computer Information Science University Macau Macau SAR ma76562@umac.mo, ccfong@umac.mo Abstract—This paper proposes data mining methodology called Business Intelligence-driven Data Mining (BIdDM). It combines knowledge-driven data mining method-driven data mining, fills gap business intelligence knowledge existent various data mining methods e- Business. BIdDM contains two processes: construction process four-layer framework data mining process. A methodology established setting four-layer framework, important part BIdDM. A case study B2C e-Shop provided illustrate use BIdDM. Keywords-Business intelligence; Data mining; BI-driven Data Mining I. INTRODUCTION e-Business rapidly evolving last two decades. Intelligent B2C recommender, smart online e- service, knowledge-driven customer-relation-management examples emergent e-Services Internet. Behind e-Services, complex networked computing systems tremendous amounts data stored databases. With growth demand, situational service comprised two disparate e-Services required, combined create new integrated experience. As such, e-Service integration eventually becomes mash-up system terms e- Service construction changes. Business Intelligence (BI) concept applying set technologies convert data meaningful information [1]. BI methods include information retrieval, data mining, statistical analysis well data visualization. Large amounts data originating different formats different sources consolidated converted key business knowledge. Figure 1 presents general view data transformed business intelligence. The process involves business experts technical experts. It converts large scale data meaningful outcomes provide decision-making support end users. On hand, DM core process transform data meaningful patterns. Rules extracted DM models heuristically output results. Traditionally, two types data mining approaches: verification-driven discovery-driven [2]. INTRODUCTION The field searching possesses text documents retrieval key features. It hard obtain specific content internet database, ease process searching taken concern. Intelligent Semantic Search The main reason applying tf/idf vector space model semantic level possibility refining query terms (concepts weights) using knowledge i.e. semantics. In classical IR query refinement process basically consists two complementary steps: query expansion term re-weighting. In first step original query may rather vague focalized towards user needs adding new terms query itself, second step already available query terms re-weighted order move new query towards relevant resources away relevant ones. The first step may sometimes involve user so-called relevance feedback, usually based correlated term finding, second strongly involves interaction users user models, requiring point retrieved resources good not. Query expansion term re-weighting could done, semantic framework, taking account semantic relationships concepts; case need correlated term finding concepts already related possible leverage ontology structure choosing new query terms. DOSE Distributed Open Semantic Elaboration platform based modular multilingual architecture, includes ontology, annotations, lexical entities search functions. The platform implemented distributed set services including: semantic annotations document substructures (e.g. chapters, sections, paragraphs), external annotation repository (based XPath XPointer technologies) automatically populated starting known ontology lexical representation concept classes, simple annotation search engine used extract recombine relevant document fragments. Annotated resources may XML XHTML static dynamic documents, need stored modified. The architecture implemented Java uses XML-RPC messages communications among internal modules external applications. The proposed semantic information retrieval system, therefore implemented language. In order assess valuability approach deployed semantic IR system defining two new DOSE modules, first implements tf/idf vector space model semantic level, second used provide “smart” functionalities query refinement based ontology navigation. Those two modules named “Basic Search” “Clever Search” respectively since DOSE architecture distributed allows concurrent access, cannot memory past queries. Therefore refinement step requires specification original query string. Fig. 4, DOSE architecture The resulting architecture depicted Fig. 5. Fig. 5, Semantic Search modules DOSE The DOSE architecture offers particular module called Semantic Mapper able map text fragment weighted set ontology concepts identifying, fragment, lexical entities associated ontology concepts. Lexical entities words word compounds usually adopted humans identify concept attached. The query must provided search engine could therefore obtained classical query string interface subsequently mapped conceptual one using module, otherwise external application charged provide query engine correct format. The Basic Search module implements tf/idf vector model search space composed annotations stored Annotation Repository. It accepts input conceptual query composed sequence {concept,weight} pairs, evaluates query annotation set ranks annotations according similarity value calculated cosine measure (1). Then selects number resources equal one required external application returns set {URI, Xpointer} pairs fragment retrieval together global relevance value associated resource. An external application already implements reasoning scheme directly access Basic Search module retrieval front-end Annotation repository, however likely applications request relevant retrieval relying query refinements automatically performed Clever Search module. The Clever Search module implements query expansion mechanism based ontology navigation. A given query first passed Basic Search evaluated following procedure described 4.3. If refinement required module first determines action must performed (6) (7) interacts ontology wrapper effective navigation. New concepts weighted exactly ones refinement started new query submitted Basic Search. The entire process iterated relevant set resources found stop criterion reached. Myers1(✉) 1 Human-Computer Interaction Institute, Carnegie Mellon University, Pittsburgh, USA 2 School Electronics Engineering Computer Science, Peking University, Beijing, China {tobyli,bam}@cs.cmu.edu, fanglin@cmu.edu yuanchun.li@pku.edu.cn Abstract. The revolutionary advances Internet Things (IoT) devices applications helped IoT emerge increasingly important domain end-user development (EUD). Past research shown end users desire create various customized automations, would often utilize multiple IoT devices. Many solutions exist support EUD across multiple IoT devices, limited devices manufacturer, within “eco- system” supporting common API. We present EPIDOSITE, mobile program‐ ming-by-demonstration system addresses limitation leveraging smartphone hub IoT automation. It enables creation automations consumer IoT devices smartphones demonstrating desired behaviors directly manipulating corresponding smartphone app IoT device. EPIDOSITE also supports using smartphone app usage context external web services triggers data automations, enabling crea‐ tion highly context-aware IoT applications. A past study shown users highly diverse personalized desired behaviors smart home automation, and, result, need end-user tools enable program environment [42]. Programming IoT Devices Demonstration Using Mobile Apps 9 4 System Design Implementation 4.1 Implementation The client component EPIDOSITE Android application written Java. It require root access Android system, work smartphone Android 4.4 above. The client component standalone. There also optional add- server application available supporting automation triggered external web services IFTTT. The server application implemented Java Jersey3 Grizzly4. The mobile programming demonstration engine used EPIDOSITE extends prior mobile PBD system SUGILITE [29]. SUGILITE uses Android’s accessibility API support automating tasks Android apps. During demonstration, user’s interactions phone, together relevant UI elements screen, recorded background recording handler accessibility API. SUGILITE processes recording generates reusable script performing task. SUGILITE also generalizes script demonstrated example analyzing user interface structures apps used demonstration. For instance, user demonstrates turn living room light using Philips Hue app, SUGILITE detect “living room” parameter script, automatically generalizes script used turn available Philips Hue lights providing diﬀerent string script invoked. With SUGILITE, user record automation demon‐ strating procedure performing task using user interfaces third-party Android app (with exceptions noted [29]), run automation multi-modal interface, invoked GUI using speech. SUGILITE also supports viewing editing scripts. Details SUGILITE found earlier paper [29]. On top SUGILITE, EPIDOSITE adds new features mechanisms support programming IoT devices smart home setting, including new ways trig‐ gering scripts, new ways scripts trigger external services devices, new mechanisms sharing information among devices. To better meet needs devel‐ oping IoT devices, EPIDOSITE also supports programming diﬀerent devices separated subscripts, reusing subscripts new scripts. For example, user demonstrate two scripts “turning oﬀ light” “turning TV”, create new script “if …, turn oﬀ light turn TV”) without demonstrate procedures performing two tasks again. 4.2 Key Features Notiﬁcation App Launch Triggers The common context-aware applications smart home naturally described using rule-based conditions model trigger-action programming, trigger 3 https://jersey.java.net/. 4 https://grizzly.java.net/. 10 T.J.-J. An IFTTT applet consists two parts: trigger action, either part EPIDOSITE script. Then, user goes IFTTT, chooses “New Applet,” chooses trigger script. After this, user chooses Maker channel action. The user paste URL IFTTT field (see Fig. 4). The procedure create EPIDOSITE-triggered IFTTT applet similar, except user needs add “trigger IFTTT applet” operation demonstrating EPIDOSITE script, use Maker channel trigger. First, executing auto‐ mation, phone must powered concurrently connected devices involved automation. If phone powered oﬀ, disconnected involved IoT devices, automation fail execute. This limitation particularly aﬀect EPIDOSITE’s applicability devices connected phone via local network short-range wireless communication technology (e.g., Bluetooth, ZigBee, IR), since devices, phone restricted connected local network, physically within range wireless connection automation work. Second, EPIDOSITE automations need run foreground main UI thread phone. Thus, automation triggered user actively operating phone time (e.g., user phone call), user’s interaction phone interrupted. The automation execution may also fail inter‐ rupted phone event (e.g., incoming phone call) user’s action. For limitations, approach use separate phone hub IoT automation, run EPIDOSITE phone instead using user’s primary smartphone. By this, separate phone consistently plugged stay connected IoT devices ensure automations triggered executed properly. Currently, compatible Android phone purchased less $50, makes solution aﬀordable. Further limitations include current version EPIDOSITE provides little assis‐ tance testing debugging. When automation uses trigger cannot acti‐ vated manually (e.g., future time, due weather condition), user may able demonstrate test automation. In case controlling app 14 T.J.-J. Li et al. IoT device updated, user may need record demonstration user interface app changed, procedure complete task diﬀerent. For reason, EPIDOSITE automation scripts may break executed environment diﬀerent IoT devices available, diﬀerent versions smartphone apps running, smartphone diﬀerent screen resolution. This limits sharing EPIDOSITE scripts, may cause runtime problems EPIDOSITE current software hardware environment changes. To ﬁx error recording, user currently either record scratch, manually edit automation script using editing panel (Fig. 1c), easy use end user. For future development, plan explore designs new end-user friendly testing debugging interfaces new error handling mechanisms automatically modify script case minor software update diﬀerent phone used. The improvements also facilitate sharing EPIDOSITE scripts among diﬀerent users. Due technical limitations current programming demonstration engine system, tasks involve use web applications supported EPIDOSITE. EPIDOSITE also yet support recording gestures sensory inputs (e.g., accelerometer, gyroscope) either, planned future. In work, enabled user trigger automations IoT devices based usage context smartphones providing notiﬁcation app launch triggers. For future work, plan generalize capabilities, design usable easily understandable ways end users create automations combining contents displayed various apps, inputs available phone sensors, available personal data phone. For example, using location sensor phone, one could enable diﬀerent automation scripts trigger depending user was. Existing context-aware computing research contributed many technical solutions transforming smartphone usage sensor data meaningful behavioral-centric personal data [43]. EPIDOSITE oﬀers opportunities connect user- centric behaviors smartphones users’ smart home environments. We hope empower end users create intelligent useful context-aware applications. We also plan explore make easier end users create complex automations control structures compound conditionals loops. AppsGate [7] supports creating control structures syntax-driven editor using pseudo-natural language, shown easy creating simple rules, diﬃcult creating compound conditionals. SmartFit [2] introduces interactive graphical editor end users create conditionals using IoT sensor data rule-based EUD systems. Ontology-driven speciﬁcation web interfaces A years ago, semantic web paradigm proposed new challenges knowledge representation, web struc- ture automation. New XML-based languages cre- ated deal semantic relationships contents dynamically generated represented web brow- ser. We ﬁrmly believe ontologies way model dif- ferent aspects user interface provide conceptual models complex relationships deﬁned. Such conceptualizations used codify high-level semantic paths automatic web-based interface genera- tion, characterization reverse-engineering pur- poses (Macı´as et al., 2006; Macı´as, 2003). Our research experience using ontologies specify knowledge building data models (domain models) used together application presentation models. This informed approach towards specifying complex knowledge focused interface’s domain presentation models, well working XML-based languages better fulﬁll assumptions knowledge distribution sharing. More precisely, work combining ontologies Model-Based User Interface (MBUI) techniques (Paterno` , 2001; Puerta Eisenstein, 1999), emerged solution claiming overcome several diﬃcul- ties automating process generating interfaces (e.g., redundancy, lack encapsulation reusability). The implicit idea behind MBUI split conceptual level user interface, leads consequently explicit speciﬁcation diﬀerent aspects interface itself, domain knowledge, presentation, dialog behavior. PEGASUS (Presentation modelling Environment Generation ontology-Aware context-Sensitive web User interfaceS) domain-independent system helps create dynamic front-end ontology-driven knowl- edge-based applications web (Castells Macı´as, 2002). The goal TUX overcome common application boundaries enabling user interaction in- formation within dedicated, contextual task environments called task containers . Human Robot Interaction Robots slowly becoming part everyday life, marketed commercial applications (viz. telep- resence, cleaning entertainment). The Human Robot In- teraction (HRI) research ﬁeld aims realizing robotic sys- tems offer interaction level much natural pos- sible (Scheutz et al., 2011). Several issues arise trans- lating human generated commands suitable robotic ac- tions. First, underlying meaning utterance needs understood, mapped robot-speciﬁc com- mands, order ﬁll gap robot world rep- resentation linguistic information conveyed user utterances. In sense, typical form semantic parsing. Semantics crucial support grounding linguistic ex- pressions objects, represented robot set beliefs (i.e. robot knowledge). The complexity task largely increases less restricted scenarios, house serving tasks, people follow a-priori known subsets linguistic expressions. This requires ro- bust command understanding processes, order face ﬂexibility natural language. In many Natural Language Processing tasks, robustness domain adaptation crucial, e.g. Open Domain Question Answering dis- cussed (Ferrucci et al., 2010), methods based Statisti- cal Learning (SL) theory successfully applied. In perspective, investigating combination different state-of-the-art textual inference technologies aimed modeling making use semantic aspects mostly relevant HRI. We started idea robotic systems ﬁrstly required exhibit two main fea- tures: order useful expected perform ac- tions take place physical environment. Mul- tiple semantic theories applied describe as- pects world taken account ro- bust HRI. In work point Frame Semantics (Fill- more, 1985) Holistic Spatial Semantics (Zlatev, 2007) relevant goal. Frame Semantics generalizes notion action making reference situation, i.e. experience usually represented Frame, i.e. micro- theory real world situation, movement actions. Holistic Spatial Semantics (Zlatev, 2007) de- ﬁnes basic concepts domain natural language spatial expressions. It helps make reference lo- cation trajectory motion, usually involving one referent discourse. In recent years adoption sound linguistic theories brought development large scale resources (e.g. FrameNet (Baker et al., 1998) Frame Semantics CLEF Corpus Spatial Semantics) support def- inition several state-of-the-art Statistical Learning ap- proaches NL tasks. However, reuse re- sources heterogeneous domain straightforward. The generalization offered ML algorithms strongly biased employed data, whose performance out-of- domain conditions may exhibit large drops. This crucial problem speciﬁc research topic, i.e. Domain Adap- tation (Daum´e Marcu, 2006), deals exactly classes problems. For example, reported (Prad- han et al., 2008; Johansson Nugues, 2008), Semantic Parsing system trained speciﬁc application-domain corpus shows signiﬁcant performance drop, applied different domains. Corpus Collection During recent years effort providing resources useful automatic understanding robot commands yielded deﬁnition corpora Natural Language HRI, (Kuhlmann et al., 2004; Tellex et al., 2011; Dukes, 2013). However, corpora highly domain sys- tem dependent. The basic idea work build corpus containing information yet oriented spe- ciﬁc application domain, e.g. house service robotics, time inspired sound linguistic theories, deﬁnition decoupled domain. The aim offer level abstraction totally indepen- dent platform, yet motivated supported the- ories. We exploited three different situations start gather- ing user utterances representing possible commands given robot house environment. Two groups utterances recorded Speaky Robots project2, third one collected interviewing mem- bers teams participating Robocup 2013 com- petition. At end gathering, three datasets collected, representing different working con- dition. Each dataset mainly characterized by: com- plexity language used user, terms de- gree variability syntactic structures lexicon; back- ground noise conditions; device used recording. Each utterance coupled correct transcription, directly inserted user operator’s control. Utterances pronounced different users, multiple spoken versions sentence included. In following, gathering process discussed three datasets. The Grammar Generated (GG) dataset contains sen- tences generated speech recognition grammar developed Speaky For Robots project. The Figure 1: The web portal used gathering crowd-sourcing generation procedure grammar presented (Car- lucci Aiello et al., 2013). The generated sentences pronounced three speakers recorded us- ing push-to-talk microphone. The acquisition process took place inside small room, thus low background noise. Moreover, push-to-talk mechanism helped precisely segmenting audio stream, reduc- ing noise. Due constrained nature, language represented free colloquial forms interaction. The S4R Experiment (S4R) dataset gathered two distinct phases Speaky Robots project ex- periment. In ﬁrst phase, users asked give commands real robot operating rooms set real home, thus capturing interferences generated talking people sounds working devices nearby. The users aware robot capabilities terms action could perform rooms objects robot able recognize. The device used GG dataset employed in- teraction. In second phase, users could access Web portal showed Figure 1 record commands. Gen- eral situations involved interaction described portal displaying text images. Each user asked give command inherent depicted situa- tion. This time internal microphone pc running portal used recording. Since users partially constrained (they knowledge capabilities robot lexicon handled Speech Recognition Engine), language represented dataset characterized features simi- lar free spoken English respect one reported GG dataset, including richer syntactic structures. The Robocup (RC) dataset collected Robocup@Home (Wisspeintner et al., 2009) competition held 2013, context RoCKIn3 project. The Web portal used S4R dataset em- ployed, recording took place directly com- petition venues cafeteria, thus different levels background noise. Again, internal microphone pc running web portal employed. Expres- sions uttered exhibit large ﬂexibility lexical choices 2http://labrococo.dis.uniroma1.it/?q=s4r 3http://rockinrobotchallenge.eu/ 4520 syntactic structures, since users received constraint command robot, ex- cept description situation. As consequence, dataset much variable representing realistic “open” application respect previous two com- mand collections. All sentences dataset anno- tated according Frame Semantics Holistic Spatial Se- mantics two annotators. POS-tags syntactic depen- dency types provided CoreNLP4 (Klein Man- ning, 2003) also validated annotation process, provided together semantic infor- mation. In last phase annotation process, tagged information validated third expert. Annotating Frame Semantics The ﬁrst aim mind realization corpus provide linguistic information spo- ken commands useful encode knowledge necessary robot fully understand them. As ﬁrst step, started considering actions robot able perform according set general commands. We decided rely frames bridge linguis- tic knowledge contained utterances robotic actions. Frame Semantics generalizes notion ac- tion making reference situation, i.e. experi- ence usually represented Semantic Frame (Fillmore, 1985). A frame micro-theory real world situ- ation: movement actions, moving, events, natural phenomena, properties, be- ing colored. A set semantic roles associated frame, i.e. descriptors different elements involved described situation (e.g. Agent movement). Linguistic resources providing information produced years, FrameNet (Baker et al., 1998). Frames interesting primitives HRI robot’s actions linked semantic frames. This semantic formal- ism already inspired RoboFrameNet (Thomas Jenk- ins, 2012) framework, set semantic frames con- textual speciﬁc robot actions deﬁned. The main difference work RoboFrameNet frames deﬁned ﬁner-grained level respect choices. Higher levels abstraction FrameNet frames allow treat general set phe- nomena settled starting point. In case, selected subset FrameNet-inspired se- mantic frames corresponding deﬁned robot actions, reported Table 3.2.. Some frames frame Scrutiny called slightly adapted, e.g. Searching. According set frames, com- mand “go near table kitchen” Motion frame evoked verb go. The frame element GOAL, rep- resenting destination motion action assigned sequence near table kitchen. The frame instantiated way ﬁnally encodes informa- tion needed robot understand action per- form, arguments involved com- mand, i.e. case object take ﬁnd it. Besides number examples se- mantic frame three datasets, Table 3.2. reports also number examples frame element according frame. Again, speakers freedom, RC dataset, expressivity grown, resulting higher number different frames related frame el- ements. Furthermore, aspect reﬂected also higher average number roles per sentence goes ∼1.5 CC dataset ∼2.0 RC dataset. The automatic annotation process decomposed Frame Prediction (FP), Boundary three subtask: Detection (BD) Argument Classiﬁcation (AC). The ﬁrst task recognizing type intended ac- tion, reﬂected events evoked targeted sen- tence, recognizing semantic frames evoked sentence; second task identifying spans arguments action, i.e. frame elements re- lated given frame; third aims assigning label span identiﬁed BD step, e.g. THEME SOURCE. The Inter-Annotator-Agreement (IAA) two annotators evaluated sub- tasks, terms Precision, Recall F-Measure. In turn tagging one annotator evaluated (thus considering second Gold Standard), mean two scores reported IAA measure. These scores showed Table 3.2.. Annotating Spatial Semantics The ﬁrst consideration arose modeling se- mantics commands Frame Semantics e GOAL frame element. related actions take place environment. We focused different spatial aspects involved interactions, spatial domain represented language. Even though Frame Semantics able capture aspects, found cases granularity level offered theory appropriate. Understanding spatial relations hold two entities conveyed natural language crucial HRI. For example, lets consider command “go near table kitchen”. Frame Se- mantics, deﬁned FrameNet, able capture relation holding table kitchen, whole sequence near table kitchen supposed considered destination motion trajectory, i.e. Identifying relation would allow robot understand table referring to, among tables robot aware of. We decided rely Holistic Spatial Semantics (Zlatev, 2007) catch phenomena. This theory de- ﬁnes basic concepts domain natural language spatial expressions. It helps make reference lo- cation trajectory motion, usually involving one referent discourse. It deﬁnes concept spatial re- lation, relation holding among different spatial roles identiﬁed sentence. This TRA- JECTOR, i.e. entity whose location relevance, LANDMARK, i.e. reference entity location trajectory motion fully speciﬁed, SPA- TIAL INDICATOR, i.e. part sentence holding characterizing nature whole relation. For exam- ple, sentence “go near table kitchen”, preposition “in” SPATIAL INDICATOR relation “table” “kitchen”, respectively TRAJECTOR LANDMARK. Spatial Semantics term three roles an- notated whole HuRIC, line annotated CLEF corpus discussed (Kolomiyets et al., 2013). It worth noting number LANDMARKs different two roles sometimes implicit, e.g. go near [the table]TRAJECTOR [on right]SPATIAL INDICATOR. Abstract Meaning Representation From considerations made previous Sections, arises two selected representations look indepen- dent properties cooperate fully express mean- ing command. They represent different forms annotation relevant expressions useful HRI do- main. In order accommodate different dimensions semantic annotation fostering adoption Abstract Meaning Representation (AMR) (Banarescu et al., 2013). AMR novel representation formalism. It allows generalize several aspects NL semantics ideal abstract aspects currently less rele- vant (such quantiﬁcation) focus spatial (and temporal) concepts, instances individual rela- tions among them. In AMR, sentences different syntactic structures, basically share meaning, seemingly expressed represented struc- ture. A major advantage robotic applications fully instantiated AMR annotation easily mapped corresponding operational commands robot, i.e. making smooth notion grounding already available. (g1 / go − Motion : Goal(i1/ − Spatial relation : trajector(t1/ table) : landmark(k1/ kitchen))) According AMR guidelines, spatial relations seen surrogate information repre- sented AMR role be-located-at. A First Empirical Evaluation As support thesis need liguistic re- sources Natural Language HRI order verify possible impact development HRI systems, ran preliminary experiments involving HuRIC. We designed processing chain able annotate Frame Se- mantics according schema described Section 3.2. The chain realized cascade processors exploit- ing different Statistical Learning algorithms, ex- plained depth (Croce et al., 2012). The Frame Predic- tion task designed multi-classiﬁcation process, us- ing SV M M ulticlass algorithm (Joachims et al., 2009). Both Boundary Detection Argument Classiﬁcation sub- tasks realized sequential labeling task SV M Hmm (Altun et al., 2003), i.e. Markovian extension SV M. In ﬁrst subtask, labeler gives label word indicating whether part frame element. In second, labeler assigns label word, corresponding associated frame element. First, trained system FrameNet corpus, evaluate performances three dataset compos- ing HuRIC (reported F N column Table 4.). In second experiment, 66% annotated examples subsets HuRIC added FrameNet ma- terial training. In way 3-fold evaluation schema enabled: cyclically, three different tests made possible remaining 33% test corpus. The macro-average achieved three measures computed ﬁnal performance, reported HuRIC column Table 4.. In labeling chain, fed module gold stan- dard input. The results reported Table 4. show adding training HuRIC results signiﬁcant im- provement performances, expecially BD AC phases. Results show percentage correctly as- signed frame (the FP column), percentage correctly recognized roles (the BD column) correctly classiﬁed respect role labels (the AC column). A con- stant improvement shown adding HuRIC annotated Table 11: Semantic Role Labeling analysis HuRIC examples, especially S4R RC dataset. It con- ﬁrms positive impact using specialized material, i.e. reﬂecting syntactic/semantic peculiarity robot com- mands. 5. Conclusion This paper presents Human Robot Interaction Corpus (HuRIC), ﬁrst collection robot commands support HRI research. First, sentences recorded audio ﬁles. Then, transcriptions annotated different kinds linguistic information, ranging mor- phological syntactic information rich semantic in- formation, according Frame Semantics Spatial Se- mantics. Finally, texts annotated way represented Abstract Meaning Represen- tation (AMR) formalism, novel ﬂexible representa- tion schema. First experimental evaluations underline positive impact resource adoption data driven methods Semantic Parsing natural lan- guage commands. Moreover, adoption open rep- resentation schema AMR enable extensions corpus according semantic theories, explicit temporal annotations. Such information could useful HRI order better support planning sequences (and individual) actions. Although semantic web standards established, applications uses data relatively limited. This partly due high learning curve efforts demanded building semantic web ontology-based ap- plications. Evolution software tool significantly profited semantic web research community Thailand contributed terms tool develop- ment adoption support. Keywords: semantic web application framework, ontology application devel- opment tool, software tool development model. 1 Introduction With Semantic Web data standards established, organizations initiatives started creating sharing data RDF format, aka. Linked data initiative [1]. Further, domain knowledge different areas increasingly captured ontology form shared OWL data linked RDF data. Although creation Semantic Web data rapidly grows, e.g. Linked data cloud [1], applications uses data relatively limited. This partly due high learning curve efforts demanded building semantic web ontology-based applications. To facilitate development applications, development tools allow application developers focus domain problems knowledge rather implementation details. Put another way, application development tools designed technologists also researchers domain experts non-technology experts. Our application framework differs existing tools two main aspects. First, framework provides common application templates process Fig. 2. A layered architecture OAM framework 1 Hozo ontology editor (http://www.hozo.jp/) used ontology development trainings. 2 OAM Homepage – http://text.hlt.nectec.or.th/ontology/ A Community-Driven Approach Development 309 user’s published RDF data. Thus, require user’s programming skill building application. Second, integrated platform supports RDF data publishing databases processing published data ontology- based applications, i.e. semantic search, recommender system applications. Fig. 2 shows layered architecture OAM framework. The framework implemented top existing semantic web data application platforms, i.e., Jena, D2RQ, RDF data storage reasoning engine. OAM added data application management functions, including database schema vocabulary ontology mapping, recommendation rule application configuration management. The user create manage ontology-based applications using web-based appli- cation templates management tools. OAM also provides APIs Web service interfaces support advanced application development. 3.1 Database Schema Vocabulary Ontology Mapping Management There typically two methods creating instances ontology classes. The first method manually construct instance define attribute values based class. This typically done using instance editor provided ontology development tool. The second method create instances existing information sources, database records. This normally requires mapping process existing database schema ontology structure [4]. After mapping process, database record transformed class instance. This method suitable organization already stored data databases. Our framework focuses latter approach creating instance data existing databases. OAM’s data mapping management tool supports schema mapping voca- bulary mapping OWL ontology relational database source. In schema mapping, user define mapping ontology classes database tables. For class, user define mapping property, i.e. either data- type property object property, database column table optional join condition. The property mapping supports one-to-one, one-to-many many-to- many relationship types databases. Based mapping configuration, tool generate instances ontology classes RDF/XML file. In vocabulary mapping, user choose table column assign attribute value label ontology class. This allow synonymous terms mapped class ontology would allow semantic-based processing applications. 3.2 Recommendation Rule Management OAM also focuses simplifying creation management recommendation rules. It provides recommendation rule management tool supports two processes: create recommendation link recommendation. Creating recommendation create recommendation class instance user define conditions class instances associated recommendation. For example, user create car recommendation instance associate car model instances match condition “a Japanese brand priced $20,000”. Linking recommendation 310 M. Buranarach, Y.M. Thein, T. Supnithi allows user define conditions class instances recommendation assigned, e.g., customer instances match condition “young adults Asian nationality”. Fig. 3 shows example resulted recommendation rules created Jena rule syntax. The tool facilitates user create business logics using form-based user interface hides complexity rule syntax processed reasoning engine. Fig. 3. An example resulted recommendation rules Jena rule syntax 3.3 Application Templates Management OAM allows user create semantic web application using provided application template. Using application template, user needs define application configuration need programming skill building application. This suitable researchers want experiment research ideas realized means semantic web technology. Application template typically ideal rapid prototyping hypotheses testing. Currently, framework provides two application templates: semantic search recommender system applications. The semantic search application template provides faceted search [5] interface. Using provided form-based interface, end-user select class ontology search instance data define search property conditions. A search allows value comparison using string number comparators semantic-based comparators, i.e. IS-A. It also allows user customize properties displayed search results. The user’s faceted search condition automatically transformed SPARQL query retrieving instance data RDF database. The recommender system application template extends semantic search template support viewing recommendation rules results. 4 Case Studies This section discusses projects adopted framework research. One project development food recommendation system [6] based user’s health status nutrition goals. In another project, OAM used support human activity recognition task smart home domain [7]. The ontology-database mapping tool helped simplify mapping process domain ontology data- base storing sensor-based data produce RDF data. The recommendation rule management component helped facilitate defining rules human activity recognition task. Finally, semantic search application template allows rapid prototyping evaluating recommendation results. Fig. 4 shows example use application framework activity recognition task smart home domain. 5 Discussion In paper, describe OAM, application framework aimed simplify development ontology-based applications use semantic web technology. Our tool development approach relied significantly contributions research community. Adoption supporting activities, trainings workshops, contributed promoting deployment software tool among community. In addition, development supporting activities, i.e. student projects coding marathon activity, allowed research community contribute improving design implementation tools. Based download statistics 2012, OAM approximately 45 downloads monthly. Approximately 200 users completed user trainings. Some future development includes adding support application templates decision support system NLP applications. We also aim improve compatibility various ontology tools add support Linked data interoperability.
